**W-M1.42's control gate, registered before W-M1.42 is built (2026-11-09 09:4x PST).** The hook briefs every request, and a request with no task in it gets a code brief matched by one common word: at 01b8ed4a (and byte for byte at the installed d4f5d05e) "stop" sends four file lines led by `internal/events/event.go` declaring `Stop`, "continue" four led by `internal/graph/knowledge/decisions.go` declaring `continues`, "yes" four led by `internal/consent/record.go`. Of the owner's 1728 prompts in 87 session transcripts, 288 are four words or fewer (280 once slash commands are dropped), and the engine sends a code brief on at least 135 of those 280 by weight, about one prompt in thirteen overall; the two harness notices that reach UserPromptSubmit as the prompt (a peer session's idle notice, a background task's completion) get one too. AC-M1.1 cannot see this: the vague rows of `edge.tsv` (K1, K2, K4) carry '-' and allow '*', so off-task lines on them are never judged. The work is W-M1.42's "target phrase of one generic token refused", and an idf floor does not express it ("continue" scores 6.97 on this tree, above most task words). The blanket rule, a brief that settles on no unit for a request that names nothing is not sent, is refused: 13 of `edge.tsv`'s 54 rows are such briefs at 01b8ed4a, and six of them (B3, C4, E3, F1, K3, N1) are task requests with a unit truth whose one to four lines AC-M1.1 does not judge, so that rule would silence them unmeasured. The gate, fixed now (5ca51b5c): `test/live/autosteer/acm11/control.tsv`, 26 of the owner's short prompts with no task content kept verbatim and the two notices, sends no brief (`TestACM11_ControlPromptsSendNoBrief`, gate 0; 20 of 28 send one at 01b8ed4a); and on `prompts.tsv` and `edge.tsv` every brief W-M1.42 silences is listed by row, a silenced row whose truth names a unit counted as a loss and read line by line.

**W-M1.42 as built: a request with no task is sent no brief (2026-11-09 19:0x PST).** Code f8c7c86f (`internal/graph/engine/notask.go`, `brief.go`). Build reads a harness notice apart from the request (`notice`): a line that opens with a bracketed label ending " notice", through the line that closes a «summary» the label's line opens, or an element whose one-word tag ends "-notification", through its closing tag. An element never closed is read as typed, and a summary never closed takes only the label's line, so a malformed envelope falls back to the base behaviour. Three shapes are refused, each leaving one decision `not sent: <rule>, <counts>` that holds no word of the request, on an empty brief the hook does not deliver (`indexBrief` returns `!b.Empty()`; the decision still reaches the evidence record): W3, the request names no unit, record or path (`Named` empty), the ladder found no unit, and it holds at most three content words, question and auxiliary words not counted; U1, one content word that is the whole target; U3, a notice with nothing after it. A unit found, kept or withheld, is never refused by W3. Three is the least bound that silences the control set; on a local list of the owner's short requests (163 distinct, not committed) it silences 115, 90 of them sent at the base, and 3 of the 8 rows labelled as holding a repo target, each naming it in a form the engine does not read (W-DEBT.8). Gate, go1.27.3 through the slot: the full gate on the commit before the amend below, with no T1c run live, and at f8c7c86f a reduced one (gofmt, vet both ways, staticcheck U1000 in nine cells, the sweep's check, RES-2, the engine package, LAW10, the dump, WM-1.42's mutant rows, clause 4) whose dump of all 736 rows is byte-identical to the full gate's, so the row readings below hold at f8c7c86f (the replay and K7 figures are the full gate's). The control gate 0 of 29 (`TestACM11_ControlPromptsSendNoBrief`; 21 of 29 sent at 01b1527e), by W3 21, U1 6 and U3 2. On the five files W-M1.42 silences exactly the registered rows, every truth column of each '-', so 0 losses: `classes.tsv` K2, K4, K7, K10; `classes2.tsv` VG02, VG03, VG10; `classes3.tsv` VG11, VG13, VG16, VG17, VG18; `edge.tsv` K2, K4; `prompts.tsv` none (K1, K5, K8, VG05 and edge K1 are refused and were already unsent at the base). Every other row of the 736 is byte-identical to the base, no table, count or calibration moves, and the registration's six task rows (B3, C4, E3, F1, K3, N1) read byte-identical, C4 silent at both. U1 silences one row the base sent, control C25. AC-M1.1 under clause 2 unchanged: prompts 10 of 10 (gate 8), edge 47 of 54 (gate 44), off-task and basis clauses 0; replay unchanged (unit at rank 1 on 64 of 513). Clause 4 met at f8c7c86f: T1's first-prompt context 589 bytes, sha256 7641dab9cde93040, the same through the hooks as at d4f5d05e; no G run has a reachable later prompt event. `TestNotice_ReadsTheEnvelopeAlone`, `TestNoTask_RefusesOnlyARequestWithNoTask` and `TestBuild_ARequestWithNoTaskIsNotSent` pass; LAW10 5 of 5; staticcheck U1000 empty in nine cells; the sweep's check 5274 mutants over 454 files ok. Mutants: WM-1.42's 50 rows red at f8c7c86f; on the commit before the amend, 171 rows over WM-1.42 (50), WM-1.41b (44), WM-1.41 (15), WM-1.40 (16), GS-1 (11), WDEBT-6 (21) and WM-1.9 (14), 170 red, the one undetected the registered WM-1.40 equivalent (the pick count is not checked). K7 on AC power with no T1c run live, a gate check only: graph total p95 89.6 ms, prompt path 121.6 ms, hook path 284.6 ms (the base's figures were read under a T1c run's load and are not compared). The full gate failed RES-2: the rule's word set was a `lazySet` built by a function literal that calls another set's `get`, and RES-2's init scan walks a literal whether or not it is called and resolves a method by name, so it reported a compile at init through `lazyRegexp.get` in `internal/steer/render/slot`, which the set never calls. The amend moves the literal into a named function with the same body and leaves the scan as it is (W-DEBT.9). In the same full gate RES-30 failed once (62 of 64 declarations landed, 2 spilled lines and 2 lock timeouts, burst 2.222 s, memory in budget at 1.139x); run alone at f8c7c86f and at 01b1527e alternately, eight times, it passed every time with 64 of 64 and no spill or lock timeout (bursts 0.49 to 1.52 s). A run alone does not reproduce the full set's load, so CI's macOS perf step, which runs the set, is the verdict. Known limit: a line that opens with a bracketed label ending " notice" is read as a notice to its end, so a request typed after such a label on the same line is dropped with it; the harness writes its notices that way, and a typed request rarely opens with one.

**T1c: a run that ends at the turn cap is judged, not voided (ruled 2026-11-09 10:1x PST, before t1c-g15 is judged; its primary unread).** t1c-g15 (G, triple 8) ended at the 150-turn cap: result subtype error_max_turns, is_error true, num_turns 151, stderr empty, no 429, cost 9.21 USD. The batch's sanity stop (the T1b adjustment, W-M1.10 above: stop before the channel check and the judge on is_error true, num_turns under 2 or a limit text) fired and printed the run as void, so r8 and u8 did not run. The registration stops the batch; the one run it voids died on the API before working (t1b-g1, HTTP 429 at turn 1). A run at the cap is an outcome of the pinned cap: the pilot judged its six cap runs as rows, T1b reads A3 to A8 against the cap, and T1c keeps the cap because a change confounds the comparison. Voiding it would censor on the outcome: the arm's longest and costliest run (9.21 USD against a G mean of 5.87) would leave the sample, with a bias of no known sign. Registered before the judge reads t1c-g15:

1. A run whose result is error_max_turns, with no 429 and no limit text, is a row: the batch's own post-sanity steps (hooks check, channel check, judge, money read) run on it by hand at the boundary, with a marker line in the rows file first. The batch code does not change mid-T1c, so a later cap run stops the batch the same way and is judged the same way.
2. A run that dies on the API (a 429, a limit text, num_turns under 2) stays void, and its name is never rerun.
3. A7 on a cap run's tree: a go test that ran and failed is the run's outcome; a judge step that failed to run (exit 127, a slot refusal) is a fault, rerun at no API cost.
4. The read prints each arm's runs at the cap beside its rate, so the censoring this rule avoids stays visible.

**W-M1.43: one reading of what a request excludes, registered before it is built (2026-11-09 13:4x PST; ordered after W-M1.42 and before `classes4`, so the held-out read measures the engine with it).** Two readers decide whether a clause excludes a unit, and they disagree.
- The name reader (`excludedClause`, `internal/graph/engine/names.go`) looks only at the clause's first word: a keep-class verb, a negation or "do not".
- The frame reads "leave" as keep (`words.go`), so "leave X alone" is a kept thing there. It bars nothing.

Probed on the W-M1.41b tree with a fixture where B374's evidence lies only in `internal/spend`:
- "B374 again, but do not touch internal/spend", "... but without touching ..." and "... but leave internal/spend alone": the clause's first word is "but", so no constraint is read. The name reader names `internal/spend`, and the brief states "unit 'internal/spend' (named in the request)".
- "B374 again. Leave internal/spend alone.": a kept constraint and no bar. The record claim states `internal/spend` ("the request names B374, which applies here").
- "B374 again, leave internal/spend alone": a candidate on `internal/spend` by its name.

Each form states or proposes the one unit the user excluded. A stated wrong unit is the error gate B weighs worst.

The change:
1. One predicate, read by both the frame and the name reader.
2. It looks past a leading conjunction (but, and, yet, though) to the clause's own first word.
3. It reads "leave X alone", "leave X untouched" and "leave X as is" as excluding X.
4. A plain keep stays a kept thing: "keep the API stable" preserves a behaviour and bars no unit. The registration writes this line with examples both ways before code.

Before code, for each row the predicate could move, the registration records at the tip whether the row names a unit and what the brief states:
- `classes.tsv` O5;
- `classes2.tsv` NE05, RF07, TO04, CC08 and KI08;
- `classes3.tsv` RF14, RF18, RF19, DL17 and TO18.

It then predicts each row's move.

Acceptance:
- the rows move only as predicted;
- no brief on any of the five files states or proposes a unit its request excludes;
- T1's first-prompt context stays 589 bytes, sha256 7641dab9cde93040;
- a mutant row for each branch of the predicate goes red.

**W-M1.43 readings and predictions, written before any code (2026-11-09 20:2x PST).** Read at 9b3248ee from the five files' dumps and the engine's source; the W-M1.41c probe build's dumps agree on every row below.

What the two readers do now:
- The name reader (`clausesOf`, `clauses.go`) cuts a sentence at commas and before an and, then, also or or that a verb or a place word follows, and drops a leading and, then, also or or. `excludedClause` then skips a clause whose first word is keep-class (keep, preserve, leave, maintain, retain, never, do; `words.go`) or a negation, and reads no name from it. A leading "but" is not dropped.
- The frame reads "never X" and "do not X" over a non-keep verb (`negatedKeep`, W-M1.35), a negation after a modal (`afterModal`) and "without X" (`splitExclusion`) as excluding X, and "leave X alone" as a kept X, which bars nothing.

The predicate after has three answers, because the name reader skips a kept clause too ("keep internal/spend stable" must not name internal/spend as the unit to change):
- Excluded: the frame bars a unit its object names (`barred`, through `unitNamed`), and the name reader reads no name from it.
- Kept: no bar, and no name read from it.
- Open: neither; the name reader reads its names.

Its rules:
- A leading but, and, yet or though is skipped before the head is read.
- A bare "not" after a skipped conjunction opens nothing: the clause is Open. "but not for Maven" (`classes2.tsv` FA10), "but not which ones it missed" (FA08) and "but not enough to tell" (`classes3.tsv` NE15) each name the gap the request fills; reading them as Excluded would bar the unit to change.
- Excluded when the head is a negation (don't, do not, never, not, without, avoid, except, excluding, skip, ignore), or "leave" with "alone", "untouched" or "as is" after its object.
- Kept for any other keep-class head, and for "leave" over the rest of a thing ("the rest of", "everything else", "all else"): that rest stays in the unit the change edits.

The keep line, both ways:
- Kept, no bar:
  - "keep the API stable";
  - "Keep every verdict identical" and "leave the protected-path and evidence checks where they are" (`classes2.tsv` RF07);
  - "Keep everything else about how the file parses exactly the same" (`classes3.tsv` DL17);
  - "leave the file-format parsing where it is" (RF18), "leave the plan construction and execution where they are" (RF19), "leave the history file holding just the history job" (RF14) and "Leave the rest of detach's reporting alone" (`classes2.tsv` DL06).
- Excluded:
  - "leave internal/spend alone", "... untouched" and "... as is";
  - "but do not touch internal/spend" and "but without touching internal/spend";
  - "Leave the hook alone" (`classes.tsv` O5) and "Leave the consent screen's wording alone for now" (`classes2.tsv` GF10).

Where an Excluded constraint reaches the claim (`claim.go`, `roles.go`):
- `barred` bars a unit whose last path segment its object holds. Of the objects that turn Excluded, two do: O5's "hook" (`internal/hook`) and GF10's "consent" (`internal/consent`). DL06's "reporting" holds `internal/report`'s segment, and the rest-of rule keeps it Kept.
- `outside` already drops every constraint's words from the ladder's context, kept or excluded; a constraint the frame did not read before takes its words out too.
- `things` keeps a kept constraint's words in the fallback phrase when the frame has no target and no addition, and drops an excluded one's. NE05 is such a frame. Its constraint's words (before, after, check) hold no package's last segment among the repository's 100, so its fallback finds no unit, as now.

The name reader changes on none of these rows: each clause it skips now it skips after, and every "but" clause among them stays Open. The frame changes on O5, NE05 and GF10 (a kept constraint turns Excluded) and may read a new Excluded constraint on DL17 and TO18 (`negatedKeep` over "don't require ..." and "never follow ..."). The frame's debug line does not print the Excluded mark (`Phrase.String`), so a unit test reads it.

Predicted per row. "Brief" is the sent lines and the judge line.

| Row | Brief now | What changes | Predicted |
|---|---|---|---|
| `classes.tsv` O5 | `internal/brief`, a candidate by its name, runner-up `cmd/rashomon` | "Leave the hook alone" turns Excluded; bars `internal/hook` | byte-identical: `internal/hook` is neither pick nor runner-up |
| `classes2.tsv` GF10 | `internal/disclose`, a candidate by its name, runner-up `internal/brief` | the consent-screen clause turns Excluded; bars `internal/consent` | byte-identical: no line names `internal/consent` (`cmd/rashomon/consent.go` is a consumer in `cmd/rashomon`) |
| `classes2.tsv` NE05 | "not known: the unit to change" | its "leave ... alone" turns Excluded; names no unit; its words leave the fallback phrase | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` DL06 | sends nothing; the ladder's `internal/store` withheld | none: "the rest of" is Kept (without the rule it would bar `internal/report`) | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` RF07 | sends nothing; the ladder's `internal/steer/render` withheld | none: both clauses Kept | byte-identical |
| `classes3.tsv` RF14, RF18, RF19 | each sends nothing; the ladder's unit withheld | none: Kept | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` TO04 | "not known" for the unit, file, consumers and records, and one D173 line; the ladder's `internal/spend` withheld | none: "never falls back ..." is cut at its "and", skipped by the name reader now, and names no unit | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` CC08 | "not known: the unit to change" | none: after "but" the head is "the", Open | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` FA08 | "not known: the unit to change" and one D149-addendum line | none: bare "not" after "but" is Open | byte-identical |
| `classes3.tsv` NE15 | sends nothing; the ladder's `internal/hook` withheld | none: bare "not" after "but" is Open | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` FA10 | "not known: the unit to change" and one D143-amended line (refused on "pass/fail", which the index lacks) | none: bare "not" after "but" is Open, and the row is refused before the frame | byte-identical |
| `classes2.tsv` KI08 | "not known: the unit to change" and record lines (refused on "D165's", W-M1.44) | none | byte-identical |
| `classes3.tsv` DL17 | `internal/spec` by coverage, runner-up `internal/disclose` | "and don't require the two fields to be set together" may become an Excluded constraint; names no unit; its words leave the ladder's context | unit, basis and band unchanged; only the runner-up and the lines under it may move |
| `classes3.tsv` TO18 | "not known" for the unit, file, consumers and records, and one D173 line; `internal/brief`, read by its name, withheld | "and never follow a symlink ..." may become an Excluded constraint; names no unit | byte-identical; a withheld line turning sent is a miss |

- Debug lines (frame, fallback phrase, ladder scores) may move on O5, GF10, NE05, DL17 and TO18, and on no other row.
- No line of any other row of the 160 per file moves. `classes2.tsv` CC10's "but not another" is inside a clause (no comma before it), so no reader sees it as a head.
- Known limits, each with a `classes4` row:
  - a request that leaves a thing alone and then changes an aspect of it ("leave X alone, but when it comes back false, have it say which keys differ", NE05) bars X's unit when X names one;
  - a negated behaviour ("never follow a symlink", "don't require the two fields") is an excluded object under W-M1.35's reading, so one whose object names the unit it changes ("never call internal/spend's lookup twice") bars that unit.
- A row that moves other than as written above is a miss, read before the code is changed again.

**W-M1.43 addendum: the rows the registration did not list, read and predicted before any code (2026-11-09 21:2x PST).** The rows above were listed by hand. A probe then ran the predicate as written over every clause of the six files: `classes.tsv`, `classes2.tsv`, `classes3.tsv`, `prompts.tsv`, `edge.tsv` and `control.tsv`, 573 rows and 1965 clauses cut by `clausesOf` (probe test sha256 afbb47b8d46f532d, row list f0dbd32d4f344a4f). It finds 48 clauses on 44 rows whose head the frame reads nothing from today and the predicate turns Excluded: "don't" 13 times, a clause-initial bare "not" 34 times on 30 rows, and "skip" once.
- The table above lists one of these clauses (`classes3.tsv` DL17's). It lists one more of the rows, `classes3.tsv` NE15, for its "but not" clause only and with no frame change, which is wrong: its "Don't change which two fields get read out of the oversized payload's head today" turns Excluded. NE15's prediction moves to the table below. The other 42 rows were not listed.
- The name reader moves on none of the 1965 clauses: it skips a "don't", "not" or "skip" clause today and after.
- The frame changes come from a stand-in. The probe rewrites each such head to a form the frame already reads ("don't" to "do not") and builds the frame. The brief cells are from the W-M1.41c dumps.
- `classes3.tsv` TO18's "never follow a symlink ..." is an Excluded constraint today, so it needs no change. `clausesOf` cuts the clause at its "and", so "never" heads it, and `negatedKeep` reads Excluded over "follow". The same holds for `classes2.tsv` TO04's "never falls back ...", CC08's "never asks it" and KI08's "never re-derived". The predicate agrees with today's frame on every "never" and "do not" clause of the six files, so TO18's byte-identical prediction holds by construction.

Rulings:
1. A clause-initial bare "not" (no conjunction before it) is read as both readers read it today. The name reader skips it, and the frame reads no constraint, bar or name from it. Whatever the predicate answers for such a clause must change nothing after it.
   - Why: English negates an imperative with "don't", "do not" or "never". A bare "not" that opens a clause does other work. It contrasts with the clause before it ("not keys", "not the redacted version"), adds to it ("not just its raw count") or states a fact ("not enough distinct days", "not an instruction"). The frame resolves no such ellipsis.
   - The evidence: none of the 34 asks for a thing to be left alone. Read as Excluded, five would bar a unit, and every bar would be false. `classes2.tsv` CC05 would bar `internal/brief`, which is in its truth. `classes3.tsv` FA13, CC15, CC17 and KI15 would bar `internal/host`, `internal/fold`, `internal/shape` and `internal/store`, each on a word used in another sense: "how many hosts", "folded into Claude's count", "its shape", "the stored record".
   - The effect: the 30 rows are byte-identical by construction, debug lines included. After a skipped conjunction, a bare "not" stays Open, as registered.
   - Known limit, with a `classes4` row: "Fix the brief, not the hook" bars nothing.
2. "don't" and "dont" read as "do not", as registered: Excluded over a non-keep verb, Kept over a keep verb (W-M1.35). Two effects follow, both as "do not" has them today:
   - The clause's words leave the ladder's context (`outside`: a constraint's words weigh nothing, W-M1.33).
   - A negated verb is a verb of its clause. Four rows' frames had no verb before: `classes2.tsv` NE02 and NE10, and `classes3.tsv` NE15 and NE18. On them, Parse's no-target switch (`frame.go`) now reads the symptom sentences' subjects as targets instead of the prompt's first subject. On all four the new phrase comes last, after the phrases read now.
   - W-M1.43 does not change the switch. A row the build shows harmed by it becomes a W-DEBT row.
3. The predicate reads "don't" spelled with U+2019 (the right single quotation mark) as "don't".
   - Neither reader knows that spelling today. A pasted "Don't touch internal/spend" in that spelling is Open and names `internal/spend`, which is the failure W-M1.43 closes, arriving by another spelling.
   - No row of the six files holds U+2019. A unit test reads all three spellings.

The order of work:
1. Before any code, the stand-in builds every row's brief at the engine of this commit. It reads each prompt with every "don't" rewritten to "do not" and every "skip" to "without", and leaves the bare-"not" clauses as written (ruling 1).
2. Every row whose sent lines move other than predicted above is a prediction miss. It is recorded beside this addendum before the code is written.
3. The built predicate's sent lines must then equal the stand-in's on every row; a difference is an implementation miss.
4. A row that moves other than as predicted is read before the code changes again.

The hand-built list was a failure, now recorded. From now on, a predicate's registration lists its rows by running the predicate as written over every clause of every file, before any prediction is written. A prediction that turns on the ladder or the unit rule is checked against a stand-in's briefs before any code.

**W-M1.47: "already" anchors the change only where a place word opens the phrase, registered before it is built (2026-11-09 22:2x PST; ordered after W-M1.46 and before `classes4`).** An anchor is the surface the change lands on (W-M1.14 R1), and a target phrase that is one overrides a named unit (`Roles.anchor`). `phraseOf` (`frame.go`) makes any phrase that "already" follows an anchor. It sets Definite from "already" first, so the definite condition its comment states is always met. The rule was built for the place form: "Show it wherever the platform obligations already appear" (PL12, T1). Elsewhere "already" marks a precedent the request points to, not where the change lands:
- `classes2.tsv` NN01: "whatever install this machine's store already points to" overrides the named `cmd/rashomon` (detachTarget) with `internal/store`, a candidate by its name;

Both rows are wrong today, and those two are the only rows on which the override fires.

The change: "already" after a phrase marks it an anchor only when a place word opens the phrase, as in the place part `splitPlace` cuts from a verb clause or a clause a place word heads. Elsewhere it marks the phrase Definite, as now. The openers "existing" and "current" anchor as now, and so does "wherever" before a definite phrase.

Readings: the anchor phrases were listed from the frame lines of the W-M1.41c dumps, the 544 dumped rows (`anchor-scan.py` sha256 1bc2bc49d1554cd4, output b60d8b524d84c9ca), and `control.tsv` holds no "already". Fourteen rows carry an anchor, and every anchor among them comes from "already":

- eleven lose it: NN01, NN08, NP01, NP08, NP09, KI03, KI04 and KI06 in `classes2.tsv`, and NN16, CC17 and KI14 in `classes3.tsv`.

An anchor is read only when a name resolves a unit, and then only among the targets. Of the eleven, only NN01 and NP09 change their claim. On NN08 and NP01 the anchor's pick is the named unit or is refused, NP08, KI14 and NN16 hold theirs outside the targets, and KI03, KI04, KI06 and CC17 resolve no unit by name.

When no unit or two or more are named this way, nothing changes. `recordUnit` itself stays as it is, since `overRecord` and `ladderStands` read it.

- KI18 claims `internal/steer`. Line 1 reads "(a candidate: the request names D181, which applies in 3 units; this one by its name)", in the unit-name cell (57 of 81). The unit turns right; truth `internal/harness;internal/steer`.

- On both, role lines take the unit-name cell's bands: targets and reuse withheld, consumers stated, knowledge a candidate. Lines 2 to 5 may move, and the judge's consumer and knowledge cells may move with them.
- Every other row is byte-identical, debug lines included.

Known limit: "AutoSteer" holds `steer` as the product's name. On KI18 that unit is the truth's, and the claim is guarded by D181's applying there. A request about the product as a whole that names a record whose evidence lies partly in `internal/steer` would claim it the same way.

Acceptance:
- the rows move as predicted, and no other row moves;
- a unit test, with a record applying in three units, reads: a request naming one unit by its name as a claim on that unit, ranked by its name; a request naming two as no claim; a request naming none as no claim; and a request barring the named unit as no claim;
- each of three mutants turns that test red: one that drops the rule, one that claims the first unit named, and one that reads the records' units without the bar;
- T1's context is unchanged.

Under option 1 as read below, with W-M1.43, W-M1.44 and W-M1.46 as predicted:
- `classes2.tsv`: named-name goes from 7 to 9 (NN01, NN02) and named-path from 9 to 10 (NP09);
- `classes3.tsv`: knowledge-id goes from 7 to 9 (KI18, KI19).

Every class on both development files would then stand at 8 of 10 or better: the bar for authoring `classes4.tsv`. NN05 and KI16 stay wrong. KI16's record, B271, applies in `cmd/rashomon` alone, so the brief states it, though the truth is the guard in `internal/selfguard`.

**AC-M1.5's ceiling under gate shape 3, read at W-M1.41c's tree (2026-11-09 20:5x PST; an input to the per-class gate ruling; no rule or truth changed).** Read on the three classes files' dumps at W-M1.41c's tree (sha256 a375b710761dc7db, cc047754de5dcc6e, 3d899fc245e57756), with simulators that reproduce the judge's per-row unit verdicts (`classes2.tsv` 123 of 160, `classes3.tsv` 130).
- Where the misses are. In the eleven classes that do not name their unit by construction (greenfield aside), a naming row is one whose prompt holds a truth unit's path, its last segment as a word, or a target file's name. Three such rows are named by a path, and 2 are right. The rest are named only by the last segment as a word: on `classes2.tsv` 28 rows, 4 right, 23 withheld by E5; on `classes3.tsv` 22 rows, 2 right, 19 withheld. A withheld line is right on a prose row and wrong on a naming row. So in these classes the unit verdict cannot exceed the prose and no-unit rows plus the naming rows the engine claims rightly. With every segment row withheld, the most a class can reach is: on `classes2.tsv` feature-add 6, refactor 5, cross-cutting 5 and question 7; on `classes3.tsv` cross-cutting 6, refactor 7 and delete 7. Four of these miss the per-class gate of 7 and all seven miss the 8-of-10 bar for authoring `classes4.tsv`, whatever else is fixed.
- What claiming them costs. The request's words do not tell the two kinds of row apart. A segment word in a request is the truth's on some rows and incidental on others, and only the truth decides which. The test rule claims the one unit a request names by word, on every row where the brief withholds and the prompt names exactly one unit, by path or by last segment as a word. On the two untuned files it is right on 25 of 71 claims (0.35):
  - `classes2.tsv`: 10 rows gained, 23 lost, unit 123 to 110 (109 when the product name is read as naming no unit);
  - `classes3.tsv`: 9 gained, 22 lost, 130 to 117;
  - `classes.tsv`: 14 gained, 12 lost.

  Kept to the ladder's pick (R1, refused above), it is right on 17 of 40 rows on the two files. No truth-free signal read on R1's 66 rows over the three files separates right from wrong:
  - the segment word's idf: rare words lean right, but `safe` at 5.94 is wrong and `store` at 2.07 is right on four rows;
  - the share of the word's symbols that lie in its unit: Go names do not repeat their package's name, so the share is 0 on 36 rows of both kinds;
  - the ladder's form, coverage and ties: on the 32 rows whose phrase has a printed ladder, the claim is by the unit's name over the whole phrase with no tie on 15 of 16 wrong rows and 13 of 16 right rows, and the margins overlap (2.9 to 261 on wrong rows, 2.7 to 78 on right ones);
  - the runner-up: a truth on 2 of 27 wrong rows.
- What the gate then asks. On these rows shape 3 owes the unit exactly when the request's word happens to be the truth's. No policy over the request's words does better than the larger of the two shares, about 2 in 3 for withholding, and either choice fails a class gate. A reader of meaning answers these rows: given the atlas, the second read's readers agreed with the authors on 135 of 160 units. The hook path holds no evidence of meaning within its budget (W-M1.37; the three probes under "What E5 costs").
- Option 1 read beside silence (2026-11-09 20:5x PST; `opt1.py` sha256 e42b0375b05f435a, output a3a3a09fb5f19817, on the same dumps). Option 1 moves 33 rows of `classes2.tsv` and 25 of `classes3.tsv` from naming to prose; greenfield's 5 and 3 among them are right either way. A brief that withholds every unit line scores 10 of 10 on each of the twelve classes that do not name their unit by construction, on both files, and 0 of 10 on the four that do. On those twelve, then, the per-class unit gate tests only that no wrong unit is stated, and each class number below is read beside that baseline. Unit right of 10 under option 1:
  - `classes2.tsv`: feature-add 9, performance 9, non-go 7, cross-cutting 9, the other eight 10 (silence 10 each); named-name 7, named-path 9, pasted-error 8, knowledge-id 9 (silence 0 each); pooled 147 of 160 against silence's 120.
  - `classes3.tsv`: performance 9, non-go 8, question 9, negation 9, the other eight 10; named-name 8, named-path 10, pasted-error 9, knowledge-id 7; pooled 149 against 120.

  The engine's lead over silence is in the four naming classes, 33 and 34 rows against 0. On the twelve others it is below silence by 6 rows on `classes2.tsv` (FA06, PF05, NG01, NG07, NG08, CC01) and 5 on `classes3.tsv` (PF14, NG11, NG15, QU12, NE12). Each of those states a wrong unit or offers a candidate pair with no truth in it, and five are non-Go requests given a Go unit (NG01, NG07, NG08, NG11, NG15; `opt1-miss.py` 53d85ec733abd86d). Under option 1 every class meets the unit gate of 7 on both files. Three are at 7, under the 8-of-10 bar: non-go and named-name on `classes2.tsv`, knowledge-id on `classes3.tsv`.
- The reuse term, which option 1 does not touch. No brief on the 480 rows of the three classes files sends a reuse line. Since W-M1.38 every reuse cell of the calibration is withheld: named 0 of 6, unit-name 1 of 8, coverage 2 of 17. The roles name reuse on 47 rows (18, 15 and 14 per file), and there the line goes with the withheld unit (28 rows) or is withheld by its cell (19); on the other 433 rows the roles name none, and no reuse line is kept on any row (`reuse-why2.py` f9ee2635bafd9325, output f87709878f72882d; it replaces `reuse-why.py` a67d0e29c899676b, cited here before, whose roles pattern stopped at the ')' in NP09's roles line and printed 14 rows for `classes2.tsv`). So the reuse term is right exactly where the truth is '-'. At W-M1.41c it misses the per-class gate on `classes2.tsv` named-path (4 of 9 judged) and knowledge-id (2 of 9), and on `classes3.tsv` knowledge-id (0 of 7); `classes3.tsv` named-name, 6 of 8, is under the bar. This is an engine gap, not an instrument one: these requests name their place, and their reuse truths are files the index holds. On 10 of the 20 knowledge-id rows a reuse truth is a file the named record applies to (`classes2.tsv` KI02, KI03, KI04, KI07; `classes3.tsv` KI11, KI12, KI17, KI18, KI19, KI20). The brief states that file today, but as the file to change (KI07: "'internal/store/scope.go' D191 applies"), a role these rows leave unjudged ('*'). A record's file read as the code to reuse would take knowledge-id reuse to 5 of 9 and 4 of 7 (KI05's '-' truth turns wrong), still under the gate (`ki-reuse2.py` 806cceed68b2e667, output 4f0d617189af0e62, over the W-M1.41c dumps). The named-path reuse truths mostly lie outside the claimed unit (`steer/decide.go`, `secretshape/redact.go`, `verify/pathclass.go`, `consent/screen.go`). No source read so far reaches 7 of 10 on either class. Three sources the index grounds were simulated on the W-M1.41c dumps under the judge's any-of rule (`sim45.py` 76a35e296ce9fd5b, output bb5127ce4aaad6ee): a second file path the request names, a file base name it names inside the claimed unit, and a cited record's file-level `applies_to`. Together they take `classes2.tsv` named-path to 6 of 9 and knowledge-id to 5 of 9, and `classes3.tsv` knowledge-id to 4 of 7, with 10 of their 15 lines on the two files naming a truth. The engine's own reuse source, withheld today, names a truth on none of the 13 rows of the two files where it was built under a claimed unit (2 of 6 on `classes.tsv`; `withheld-reuse.py` c91c65716299b643, output 1e4fec3b07e0be7a), so re-reading its calibration would not help. A weaker gate would not close it either. Under a reuse analogue of shape 3, where reuse is owed only on a row whose request names a truth file (its path, base name or stem, or a cited record that applies to it), the same three sources give 7 of 9, 7 of 9 and 4 of 7 (`shape3r.py` 969fbb03ee70940c, output 6c271f20c0f5a852). The truths these rows miss are named by what the code does ("that same draw", "the same append chain", "the existing probe"), which the index's names and record links do not reach on these rows. So the reuse term needs a source that reads what code does. The index already holds one: 4323 of its 6030 symbols carry their doc comment, and the reuse source counts a doc's word only when some symbol's name holds it. An untuned probe ranks files by the request's words in their symbols' docs and names. On the 23 rows the term misses, it puts the truth first on 5 and in the top three on 10 (`docrank.py` 17ffda4baa66d13a, output 66ab846cdfd48c6a), including `classes2.tsv` NP03 and KI01, which no name or record reaches. It is a lead, not a source. It ranks some file on every request, so it needs a basis for when a request asks to build on existing code, registered with predictions before any code. The index-time model of option 2 is the fallback if the docs do not reach the gate, and it must keep INV-6 (the shipped binary links no networking package).
- The ruling's options:
  1. Judge a row named only by a segment word, in a class that does not name its unit by construction, as a prose row. This is a further post-hoc change to the gate, recorded as one, and it narrows M1's unit claim to the bases a request names. Prose recall moves to the milestone that builds section B1's proposer (model at index time, data at hook time), with these readings as its floor to beat.
  2. Keep the gate and build B1's proposer inside M1. That needs a model at index time and is days of work; its effect on these rows is unmeasured. It reopens ruling 9 (section 13), which keeps the model-assisted proposer closed until after M5.
  3. Keep both, and record AC-M1.5 as not met at close.
- Recommended: option 1. As written, the gate owes on these rows what the request does not say. Withholding there meets outcome B ("avoid false-positive guidance"), and section B1's proposer is the design the plan already holds for prose recall. Option 3 leaves M1 open on a gate the hook path's word evidence cannot meet. Option 1 alone does not close AC-M1.5. The reuse term stays open on three class cells, and since that is a capability the engine owes, no ruling on the gate takes it away. Its fix needs a source that reads what code does, registered with predictions before any code; the path, base-name and record sources stop at 6 of 9, 5 of 9 and 4 of 7, and the symbols' doc comments are the lead (the reuse term, above). (Amended 2026-11-09 21:3x PST.) The doc-comment lead puts the truth first on 5 of the 23 missed rows, so it does not reach the gate either. The three grounded sources mostly restate a file the request already names, or relabel a file the brief already states. They would raise the cells without telling the agent anything it lacks, and they still miss. So the reuse term needs a source that reads what code does. In this plan that source is B1's proposer, which ruling 9 keeps closed. The term then has two ways out:
  - ruling 9 is reopened for a reuse proposer inside M1;
  - the term is recorded as not met at M1's close and owed by the proposer milestone, whose AC-P1 gains a reuse arm with these 23 rows as the floor to beat.

  Recommended: the second. The brief sends no reuse line today, so it gives no false guidance there (outcome B). The value AutoSteer is built on, fewer repeated mistakes, does not wait on it. Ruling 9 already orders the proposer after M5, and AD5 keeps it opt-in with zero tokens by default.

It was chosen after both development files were read, and it credits withholding where a segment word names the unit, so `classes4.tsv` is its only held-out test.

Primary (A1a all-four) with 95 percent Wilson intervals: U 1/10 (0.02-0.40), R 2/10 (0.06-0.51), G 4/10 (0.17-0.69); R minus U +0.10, G minus R +0.20; G0 1/3 beside (0.06-0.79). No lift claim: each pair of intervals overlaps (U's upper bound 0.40 lies above G's lower bound 0.17). At n=10 two Wilson intervals separate only at a difference of six runs or more (0 of 10 against 6 of 10), and from U's 1 of 10 it would take 8 of 10. Fisher's exact test, two-sided (acm14/fisher.py, sha256 6390ad483f266eb3, its checks PASS 3 of 3): G against U 4/10 vs 1/10 p = 0.3034; G against R 4/10 vs 2/10 p = 0.6285; R against U 2/10 vs 1/10 p = 1.00. Two 95 percent intervals that do not overlap is a stricter condition than a test of the difference; the exact test is the direct statement, and it supports "no lift claim" only: p = 0.30 is not evidence that the arms are equal. Cost per run is close across the arms with hooks (R 5.97, G 5.91 USD), so cost per correct run moves with the primary's pass count and is no separate evidence.

**W-M1.41c: a named record that applies in the ladder's pick settles the claim, registered before it is built (2026-11-09 14:5x PST; ordered after W-M1.42 and before W-M1.43 and `classes4`).** W-M1.41b makes a record claim only when the record's unit differs from the ladder's pick (`overRecord` returns "" when they are the same unit). When the request's words and its named record point at the same unit, the brief says less than when the record alone points there:
- it reads "a candidate by word coverage" or "a candidate by its name", and never names the record as the reason;
- on 7 rows it also says "not known: the records that apply to the change", although the request named one that applies in that unit.

Counted at the W-M1.41b tree:
- 10 rows have a claim on a named record's unit that is not a record claim, and all 10 claim a right unit:
  - `classes.tsv` N2, N3 and N9;
  - `classes2.tsv` KI01 and KI04;
  - `classes3.tsv` KI11, KI13 and KI17;
  - `edge.tsv` N2 and N3.
- 7 of them say the records are not known: N2, N3, KI01, KI04 and KI11, and edge N2 and N3.

The change: when a named record's evidence lies in the unit the ladder picked, the claim is a record claim on that unit, with the forms and bands of W-M1.41b.
- One unit, form (a): stated in the named cell.
- Several units, form (b): banded by the ladder pick's basis.

Before code, the registration predicts each of the 10 rows: basis, band, the unit line's text and its byte length. It also predicts every move on the stated and candidate counts and on the calibration cells.

Acceptance:
- no unit moves;
- the "records not known" lines go from 7 to 0 on these files;
- every other row is byte-identical;
- stated precision on each file stays at or above the stated floor (0.8);
- T1's first-prompt context stays 589 bytes, sha256 7641dab9cde93040;
- a mutant row for each branch goes red.

All 10 rows are in-sample, so `classes4` is the held-out test of this rule.

**W-M1.41c as built: a named record that applies in the ladder's pick settles the claim (2026-11-09 20:5x PST).** Code 450b9db1 (`internal/graph/engine/record.go`, `roles.go`). When the phrase's pick stands against `overRecord` and the named records' unit is the pick's, `ladderStands` reads the records first:
- in several units: form (b), the pick is their claim, banded in the ladder's cell, with the next ladder pick on one of their units as runner-up and every node kept;
- in that unit alone: form (a), the pick is their claim in the named cell, keeping only the pick's evidence they apply to (`held`: all of it when one applies to the unit as a whole, else the nodes in a file one applies to);
- on none of that evidence: the claim on their own evidence takes the unit after the phrase loop, so no line in the named cell rests on the words alone.

The registered terms, each read at 450b9db1:
- no unit moves: the 10 registered rows move and no other, in basis and band only (`classes.tsv` N2, N3, N9; `classes2.tsv` KI01, KI04; `classes3.tsv` KI11, KI13, KI17; `edge.tsv` N2, N3); `prompts.tsv` and the fix set do not move;
- "not known: the records that apply to the change" on these rows goes from 7 to 0. On 5 of the 7 (`classes.tsv` N2, N3, `classes2.tsv` KI04, `edge.tsv` N2, N3) the line now reads "not known: ... other records that apply to the change". That is W-M1.41b's form when a withheld knowledge line is not one of the claim's records: the request's record is stated and only the others are withheld;
- every other row is byte-identical;
- stated precision, floor 0.8: `classes.tsv` 39 of 39 to 40 of 40, `classes2.tsv` 33 of 37 to 34 of 38 (0.895), `classes3.tsv` 35 of 41 to 37 of 43 (0.860), `edge.tsv` 31 of 33 (coverage 5 of 5 to 3 of 3, record 1 of 1 to 3 of 3, lowest cell unit-name 10 of 12). N9, KI01, KI11 and KI13 move from candidate to stated, so candidate lines holding a truth drop with them (`classes.tsv` 38 of 41 to 37 of 40, `classes2.tsv` 11 of 13 to 10 of 12, `classes3.tsv` 7 of 9 to 5 of 7). **M2 opened at 072e3e36 (2026-11-11; the director's rulings, message of 2026-11-11T15:39:02.319Z (07:39:02 PST), on the owner's word of 2026-11-11T15:26:00.885Z (07:26:00 PST), transcript 7909eedf line 4853: "merge, and get M2 moving"; a ruling by the owner replaces any of them).** "merge" is the merge of PR #283; "get M2 moving" answers the director's offer to draft M2's opening (time box, gate, what not met does, all registered before the first card) with the two M1 debts as its first cards. Registered before M2's first card:

1. Time box. M2 closes on Wednesday 2026-11-18 at 17:00 PST, on the readings in hand.
2. Gate. AC-M2.1 to AC-M2.4 as section 8 words them at 072e3e36, each read once at the close at one frozen engine sha, plus the demonstration gate of 5.2's M2 entry (T2). A row's wording changes only by a ruling recorded with its date before that row's read.
3. Not met. A row not met at the box's end is recorded not met with its numbers. It is not repaired and not read again inside M2. It becomes a W-DEBT row naming the milestone that owes it. M3 opens the same day.
4. First cards. W-DEBT.14, then W-DEBT.13, on AC-P1's terms as section 8 words them: a fresh set authored after the change is frozen, read once, on a gate registered before the set is authored that the all-silent brief fails. Inner box: that one read is done by Friday 2026-11-13 at 23:59 PST. If it is not done by then, or if it reads not met, the row is recorded and stays owed by AC-P1 unchanged. There is no second set inside M2, and the engine lane moves to W-M2.0.
5. The fresh set. The `classes4.tsv` protocol whole (160 rows, 16 classes, second-read truths), so the instruments are the ones M1 already validated. The gate has three parts, drafted by QA and ruled by the director, and is registered here before the set is authored: (i) the unit term on named-name, read5's eng-o1 at 7 of 10 or more, as registered; (ii) the reuse term on named-name, named-path and cross-cutting, its denominator fixed by the truth alone (every row of the class whose truth holds a reuse file), so that the all-silent brief scores 0 and fails; (iii) a guard that the change states no more wrong units than the engine at 23fc0483 does (`classes4.tsv`: 35 stated, 1 wrong). Beside every gate figure, the all-silent brief's score on it; for each truth, how it is authored without reading the relation the changed rule reads.
6. Development data. `classes.tsv`, `classes2.tsv`, `classes3.tsv` and `classes4.tsv` are development data. No engine change is judged on them as acceptance.
7. Cut order if the box runs short. First W-M2.7. Then W-M2.9 and W-M2.10, carried under W-DEBT.16. Then T2 from n=10 to n=3, with AC-M2.3 recorded not met at the count run. W-M2.0 to W-M2.6 are not cut.
8. Branch. M2's integration branch is `autosteer/m2`, created from main's merge commit of PR #283, or from 072e3e36 if the merge has not landed when the registration commit is ready (main is then merged in at the first task boundary under 10.3). Task branches are `as/m2-<task>`. `autosteer/contracts-v2` stays at 072e3e36 as M1's record. `as/m2` (489a4655) and `as/m2-overlay` (cb3d4296) hold docs only from 2026-11-05; they are retired once their block is ported, and not deleted. 10.3's first bullet is amended in the same commit.
9. The debts' gate, as ruled. It refines ruling 5 in four places: term (ii) is pooled, guard (ii-b) is added in an absolute form, term (iii) reads against a control column, and a spend rule decides whether the set is authored at all.
- The set. One fresh set F (`classes5.tsv`) by the `classes4.tsv` protocol whole, as QA's draft words it under "THE SET": the same authoring rules, atlas and knowledge records, one Sonnet author per class, a blind second read by three readers per row, adjudication by fixed rules; the taken-requests list gains `classes4.tsv` and the ids run XX31 to XX40. It is read once, by Friday 2026-11-13 23:59 PST (ruling 4's inner box). F is authored only when (a) a change is frozen by Thursday 2026-11-12 18:00 PST, (b) the spend rule below is cleared, and (c) the owner has said yes to the ruling 11 proposal.
- Term (i), the unit term on named-name: read5's eng-o1 at 7 of 10 or more, as registered.
- Term (ii), the reuse term, pooled: D = D_nn + D_np + D_cc, the rows of named-name, named-path and cross-cutting whose reuse truth is a file (neither '-' nor '*'), fixed from the truths alone at seal; met at ceil(0.7 x D); each class's cell printed and not gated. Because no reuse change enters this box, the term is recorded on F's read as "no change frozen": 0 of D, with D from the seal check, and one check on the read's dump that every `:147` line reads "reuse []". If any line does not, the director rules before anything is recorded. reuse6.py is not built in this box; it belongs to the card that next attempts W-DEBT.13.
- Guard (ii-b), absolute, because "no more than the control" against a control that delivers nothing would mean zero: of the reuse lines the change delivers on rows of the three classes whose reuse truth is a file or '-', at least ceil(0.7 x delivered) name a truth file. At 0 delivered the guard is vacuous and the record says so. False reuse lines over all 160 rows are printed beside the control's.
- Term (iii), the guard on wrong stated units, with a control column: 23fc0483 (tree 3e45e8f6) runs as a control column in the same slot run. Met when wrong(change) is no more than wrong(control) over F's 160 rows and gate B's 0.8 floor holds at the change. The control column is not a second read of the gate. Printed beside, not gated: stated and wrong for both binaries, and the right share of the units the change adds.
- The judge. The judge's fresh-set name changes (`classes4.tsv` joins the names it refuses) and read5's stop tests are re-run; both are registered and proven before F is authored, on a task branch cut from `autosteer/m2` after this text's commit, on the director's word.
- Seal check. From F's truths alone, before the read: D_c and the pooled D, silence's score on (i), (ii) and (iii), and the thresholds. D must be at least 3 and silence must fail (i) and (ii); otherwise the director rules before the read.
- Spend rule (the director's, sent to the engine lane 2026-11-11 08:29:05 PST: after card 0's mutants A and A47 were read, and before the candidate rules K1 to K3 were written, `predictions-K.txt` born 08:38:57 PST; development data, not acceptance). F is authored only if the frozen change, on each of the four development sets of ruling 6, (1) states no more wrong units than 23fc0483 (0, 1, 5, 1), and (2) reads named-name at 7 of 10 or more on `classes4.tsv` and no lower than 23fc0483 on every other set that has the class, by read5's eng-o1. QA computes it from the implementer's sealed dumps; the engine lane does not grade its own change. If eng-o1 and the `:147` unit verdict differ on any set, both are reported and the director rules. The script is proven on two known answers before it is used: base must print 0, 1, 5, 1 and `classes4.tsv` named-name 5 of 10; card 0's A47 dumps must print 5, 4, 9, 9, named-name 8 of 10 and "bar not cleared". If no change clears the bar by the freeze, no set is authored, both debts are recorded "no change frozen", and the engine lane moves to W-M2.0 under ruling 4.

If F is not authored, the spend rule's record stands ("no change frozen"); if F is authored and not read inside the inner box, ruling 4's record stands. W-DEBT.13 (AC-P1's reuse arm) is recorded "no change frozen" whatever (i) and (iii) read, and stays owed by AC-P1 unchanged. **M2 opened (2026-11-05 20:4x PST, the owner's word "kick off M2"; session 28d65377 renamed M2; branch `as/m2` from 33b9d8b4 in the worktree `/Users/karthik/src/rashomon-wt/m2`).** Intentional change (owner's word, 2026-11-05 20:4x) from this section's rule that step-level detail follows the slice's read: T1b stood at u1 and r1 valid, g1 void and g2 running when M2 opened, so every item below that T1b's read would shape is marked PROVISIONAL and is re-ruled at the n=3 read before its first test is written; the interfaces (W-M2.0) and the facts are not provisional. The integration branch stays `autosteer/contracts-v2` and the T1b batch runs from its worktree, so no file there moves until the batch's last judge ends; `as/m2` merges into it after the T1b read under the section 10.3 gate; the plan mirror under `~/.claude/plans` tracks the integration branch and never `as/m2`, so PLAN.md on `as/m2` is ahead of the mirror until that merge (recorded here, not drift).

Facts read at 33b9d8b4 that fix M2's shape (each a code read, none assumed):

- `spec.RuleKind` is the closed list forbid_code, forbid_command, protect_path, require_evidence, order (`internal/spec/spec.go`); `spec.Rule` is {ID, Kind, Why, HardControl, Alternative, Pattern, Languages, Paths, ExcludePaths, Command, Modes} (`internal/spec/file.go`); `internal/spec` imports nothing of the tree, so it stays a leaf and gains fields, never an import.
- Import directions: `internal/graph/knowledge` imports `internal/graph` and the slot package; `internal/verify` imports spec, steer, store and shape and never `internal/graph`; `internal/graph/engine` imports graph, pattern, render and slot and never spec; no file under `internal/hook` imports `internal/graph`. These four directions decide where M2's code lives (LLD below).
- `verify.Obligation` is {ID, Scope, Kind, Source, State, Evidence []int64}; kinds gate_after_write, watched_red_then_green, exit_unmasked, evidence_bound, spec_rule, platform_impact, expected_evidence, proposed_check; scopes session, subtask, commit; the ledger is computed by one producer at Stop from the record (`ledgerOf`), never accumulated between hooks.
- `engine.ConflictKind` has one value, arity; `engine.Conflict` is {Kind, Edge, Callee, Want, Got, Rev}; `ArityConflicts` and `CallerConflicts` exist with tests only; `engine.Diff`, `UnitBrief`, `Deltas` and `Conflicts` do not exist. `Build` is Scope (SeedFloor 1.0, MaxSeeds 8), Traverse (Depth 2), Rank, Select (MaxLines 5, dropRepeatedHubs), lineFact, bind, deliver; `Options` is {IDF, Rel, Lessons, Lexicon}.
- `graph.Store.ApplyOverlay(file, content, recs, rev)` and `Overlays()` exist with no caller outside graphtest; `lang.Resolver.Overlay` exists; `gopg.Overlay` returns nil, nil. No overlay is applied on any hook path today, and a hook is a fresh process per event, so an overlay applied in memory is gone at the next event: W-M2.5 is the parser and the journal that carries overlays between processes, larger than its table row.
- The post path: `cmdPost` (`cmd/rashomon/post.go`, 48 code lines) builds `hook.NewPost` and calls `Post.Steer(env)`, which judges, joins the blast-radius draft to the findings (`blast`, `internal/hook/steerstep.go:143`), routes (`steer.Route`) and settles the draft (`settleBlast`, :161). The prompt path's graph seam is cmd-level: `graphBrief` then `graphFinding` appended under the `graphed` guard (`cmd/rashomon/preflight.go:93`, :128). M2's first-write finding takes the same seam shape at cmd level and `internal/hook` stays graph-free.
- `steer.Finding.Key` dedupes within one prompt (`internal/steer/finding.go:53`, `route.go:76`): once-per-session needs its own evidence, and the steer log already holds it (every delivered line with its key).
- hooks.json installs PreToolUse and PostToolUse with matcher `*` at 5 s; `hookio.EventKind` maps both; `events.Kinds` holds plan with no producer. The ExitPlanMode tool call's tool_input holds the keys `plan` (the plan's markdown; 119,885 bytes in this session's own call) and `planFilePath`, read from this session's transcript at Claude Code 2.1.280 at zero cost; section 15's item "W-M2.6: the ExitPlanMode payload" is closed.
- The hooks reference (code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, read 2026-11-05 20:3x) lists `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` under PreToolUse's output, and the 2.1.280 finding behind R5 says the field is not carried. Unreconciled; W-M2.6 opens with a one-call probe (a PreToolUse hook returning the field, the transcript read for its attachment) and R5 stands until the probe says otherwise.
- Budgets: `test/perf/budgets.json` governs the post path by per_tool_call (379 MiB.ms, 32 MiB summed peak, 6 process creations) and seven post rss_cases; W-M1.8 measured the prompt hook at about 39 MiB peak with this repository's 8.5 MB graph loaded, about 30 MiB for the load alone. A graph load at PostToolUse would break the 32 MiB summed peak by itself. This number decides W-M2.3's design.
- LAW10 code lines at 33b9d8b4: `engine/conflicts.go` 64, `verify/obligations.go` 295, `spec/spec.go` 97, `cmd/rashomon/post.go` 48, `preflight.go` 163, `graph.go` 303, `hook/blastradius.go` 161; new behaviour goes to new files.
- No `.golangci.yml` exists in this repository, so W-M2.7's depguard reader is tested on a fixture, and this repository's own declared boundary (AC-M2.1) is a spec.json rule.

Design lines (the five the review reads):

- HLD: Check Conflicts is a pure function over the graph at a rev, a Diff and rules, producing Conflicts; the hook path turns conflicts into findings at nudge and the Stop turns them into obligation rows; nothing blocks (R2) and no model is called (D160). Three moments gain code: plan (W-M2.6), first write in a unit (W-M2.3), post-act and Stop (W-M2.4). Overlays (W-M2.5) make the graph at the act's rev the thing checked.
- LLD: `engine.Rule` is the engine's own small type and cmd converts `spec.Rule` to it, so the engine imports no configuration package; `knowledge.Rules` compiles knowledge rows into `spec.Rule` (knowledge imports spec, which imports nothing); `verify.Obligation.Unit` is a string global id so verify stays free of `internal/graph`; the first-write and plan findings are composed in cmd and joined to the post step through `hook.SteerEnv`, the shape the blast draft already has.
- Algorithms: boundary_cross is a prefix match of unit ids over the diff's new cross-unit edges (no regexp, RES-2); decision_contradicted is a set intersection of a rule's applies_to with the diff's touched ids plus the named predicate; duplicate_abstraction is stem overlap (`engine.Tokens`, the M1 CamelCase rule) between an added symbol's name and doc and existing symbols with callers, threshold pre-registered after a replay over this repository's merged diffs (PROVISIONAL); signature_mismatch wraps `ArityConflicts` at the overlay rev; `UnitBrief` is Traverse at depth 1 from the unit over In edges with Rank and Select unchanged; `Deltas` is a set difference on line keys.
- Patterns: pure core with adapters at the edges (the engine pure, cmd converting); write at the expensive moment and read at the cheap one (unit briefs precomputed at the prompt, where the graph is already loaded, and read as one small file at PostToolUse: memoization across processes); an append-only journal replayed on load (overlays); the steer log as the single source of "already delivered" (a fold, not a second store).
- Standards: LAW10 by new files; RES-2 (prefix matching, no package-level regexp); D160 (plan text and edit bodies read in memory, nothing of them persisted; identifiers and doc sentences are the structure class and may be stored); D161 (truth-table rows of PF-SP and PF-IV touched by M2 travel with the PR); disclosure paragraphs for the overlay journal and the unit-brief files; `TestEngine_NoLanguageLiterals` and `TestCore_NoAgentKeys` stay green; a budgets row for every hook-path change; sweep anchors for every new guard.

Ordered tasks with files, interfaces and tests by name. The five-step shape of 6.1 applies to every task: write the named tests, run them through the slot and watch them fail, implement, run the package and the standing gate (the whole tree), commit on `as/m2`.

**W-M2.0 Interface commit (new row; 0.5 day; one commit, type and test only, no behaviour).** Added so the lanes and M3 build against frozen types (Part C7, item 2), the rule W-M1.7 set for M1. Produces: `spec.RuleKind` values boundary, decision, reuse and `spec.Rule` fields `From []string`, `To []string` (unit-id prefixes), `Predicate string` (paths_untouched, symbol_present), `AppliesTo []string` (global ids), `Source string` (the knowledge row id); `engine.Rule{ID, Kind, From, To []string, Predicate string, AppliesTo []graph.ID, Source string}`; `engine.Diff{Rev graph.Rev, Touched []graph.ID, Files []graph.ID, Decls []graph.Record, Removed []graph.Record}`; `engine.ConflictKind` values boundary_cross, decision_contradicted, signature_mismatch, duplicate_abstraction, protected_path beside arity, and `engine.Conflict` gaining `Unit graph.ID`, `RuleID string`, `Subject graph.ID`, `Evidence []graph.ID` (additive; the arity tests stay green); `verify.Obligation.Unit string` (json unit, omitempty); `hook.SteerEnv.Join []steer.Finding`. Files: `internal/spec/kinds.go` (new), `internal/graph/engine/diff.go` (new), `conflicts.go` (kinds), `internal/verify/obligations.go` (one field), the file `SteerEnv` lives in (named at step 1). Tests: `TestRuleKind_ClosedListGrowsByThree` (the three kinds parse; an unknown kind is refused as today), `TestConflictKind_ClosedList`, `TestObligation_UnitRoundTrips`, and `TestFacts_JSONTagsMatchSpec` with the key-path golden unchanged (the steer line does not change in M2). Gate: the whole packages spec, graph/..., verify, hook, steer/... and the acceptance package through the slot.

**W-M2.0 as built (2026-11-11): facts re-read, rulings, known limit.** Facts re-read at bcedfbf3 (2026-11-11), in `docs-out/evidence-m2/m2-w2.0/facts-reread.txt` (sha256 dbfd4d283e004078b26863c71abf087e37a85ba05463848eaf74210592fdb6e5), accepted by the director the same day (ruling C). MIS-READ means the code is unchanged since 33b9d8b4 and the fact did not match it there either; MOVED means the code changed after 33b9d8b4.
- MIS-READ, the rule-kind list: two lists existed, `spec.RuleKinds` (spec.go:44) and parse.go:145's private `ruleKinds` in another order, and Parse validated through the second (parse.go:478).
- MIS-READ, `spec.Rule`: 19 fields, not 11; the fact omitted At, Before, Evidence, Then, After, AfterGate, SameRunKey and InvalidatedBy (file.go:72-97).
- MIS-READ, verify's imports: also baseline, gateout, steer/pattern, steer/render and steer/render/slot; "never internal/graph" holds.
