- MOVED, engine's imports: also internal/graph/lang (names.go:11).
- MOVED, engine Options: unexported bands (:62) and unitRule (:66) added (W-M1.38).
- MOVED, knowledge: KindRequirement added (W-M1.12). W-M2.1 selects by status and predicate; whether an approved requirement row with a predicate compiles to a rule is ruled at W-M2.1's open (ruling E2).
- Rulings (the director's, 2026-11-11; an owner ruling replaces any):
- D1. One list of rule kinds: `spec.RuleKinds` is the single source and Parse validates through it (`ruleKindNames`, kinds.go, converted per call under RES-2); parse.go's `ruleKinds` is gone. The fixed-string grep before the change, with a control that hit (parse.go:145), found no test or golden that pins the refusal's kind order: the refusal names no list (error.go:60-68).
- D2. Files: internal/spec/kinds.go (new), internal/spec/spec.go, internal/spec/file.go, internal/spec/parse.go, internal/graph/engine/diff.go (new), internal/graph/engine/conflicts.go, internal/verify/obligations.go, internal/hook/steerstep.go; the table row's Files cell lists these eight. spec.go joins the cell by the director's ruling (2026-11-11), because RuleKinds, D1's single source, is declared there; the three constants and the list grow in place. parse.go's net change is zero (466 code lines before and after); the five new fields are read in kinds.go (`ruleRefs`).
- D3. Type and test only: the named tests and no more; the build is the proof that no == or map key uses engine.Conflict (now non-comparable: Evidence is a slice). Build result: `go build ./...` exit 0, 0 B of output.
- D4. Carried to W-M2.2: conflict kinds with no edge share one Key() (conflicts.go); before such a conflict enters session.go's map, Key() tells them apart, pinned by a named test. Carried with it, the three readers whose kind switch the new kinds fall through: intent.go:195 (Resolve names any rule whose Paths cover the prompt's file, whatever its kind), recap/rules.go:75 (ruleWhat's default) and verify/tosteer.go:163 (ruleShapeFits's default).
- D5 (the director's, 2026-11-11 14:27, at W-M2.2). The frozen `engine.Diff` gained one field, `Base graph.Rev`, which DiffOf sets. P2 ruling 3 decides Decls and Removed by identity alone, which removed the only channel a record changed in place had to its base version (its old record in Removed); signature_mismatch reads a callable's sig at d.Rev and at d.Base. Explicit, not derived from d.Rev.Commit; additive. A Diff built by hand leaves it zero and the signature detector compares nothing (the control in `TestConflicts_SignatureMismatchWrapsArity`); DiffOf's setting is pinned in `TestDiffOf_AddedAndRemovedDecls`.
- engine.Rule (the director's ruling, 2026-11-11): ID and Kind are string, as spec.Rule's are; the grouping `ID, Kind, From, To []string` in the paragraph above is prose, not the types.
- Predicates: `spec.RulePredicates` is the closed list of decision predicates, `PredicatePathsUntouched` ("paths_untouched") and `PredicateSymbolPresent` ("symbol_present"). Data only: Parse checks Predicate's shape, not membership; the membership check lands with W-M2.1's compiler. Pinned in `TestRuleKind_ClosedListGrowsByThree`.
- Known limit: `spec.UnenforcedRuleKinds` is boundary, decision and reuse, the kinds Parse accepts that no evaluator enforces. A rule of one of them changes no verdict, ledger row, finding or hit, with paths or without (`TestRuleKinds_UnenforcedChangeNoVerdictOrFinding`, internal/verify); the prompt reader names it when its Paths cover the prompt's file, as for any path rule, and not otherwise (`TestRuleKinds_UnenforcedIntentNamesOnlyWithPaths`, internal/intent). Each test carries a control of an enforced kind that must change its read. Two tests, not one: verify's run fixture (`runOf`) is package-internal and keeps the hook's record invariants, and a verify test cannot import intent, which imports verify. Each card that enforces a kind deletes it from the list in the same commit; M2's close reads the list.
- Sweep anchors: VER-37's "rule kind order is refused" and "rule kind forbid_file is accepted" keyed on parse.go's `ruleKinds` literal, which D1 removed; both now key on spec.go's RuleKinds (`sweep.py --check`: 5,274 rows in 454 files, every anchor once).

**W-M2.1 as built (2026-11-11).** `knowledge.Rules(rows []Row) ([]spec.Rule, []Refusal)` in `internal/graph/knowledge/rules.go`. It returns the refusals as a list, in input order, so the compiler stays pure (no I/O, no clock) and a caller or test can count what was not compiled. A `Refusal` names the row, the predicate and a reason. A row compiles only when its status is approved, its tier is DECLARED, its kind passes `rowKindCompiles` and a predicate in `spec.RulePredicates` is named; one decision rule is written per such predicate, in input order, with `Source` the row id, `AppliesTo` the row's `applies_to` and, for `paths_untouched`, `Paths` the predicate's argument. A candidate, an INFERRED row and a row with prose and no predicate compile to nothing and are not refusals; there is no prose parsing. Nudge-only is by kind: a compiled rule is a decision, and `spec.UnenforcedRuleKinds` (kinds.go:20) lists decision, so no evaluator enforces it until a later card enforces the kind and deletes it from that list; no spec field makes a rule nudge-only, and `HardControl` is required prose (file.go:80, parse.go:479). `Why` is the row's clause, else its title, without control characters and cut to `spec.MaxWhyRunes`; `HardControl` is the one fixed sentence "rashomon compiled this rule from approved knowledge row <id>; it is a nudge and blocks nothing". A compiled rule's kind comes from the predicate, never from the row kind, so every compiled rule is a decision. Decision rows only compile in M2, through the one switch `rowKindCompiles`: an approved DECLARED row of any other kind that names a known predicate (a requirement row, for one) yields a `Refusal` with the reason "row kind not compiled" and no rule, so an approved check the binary ignores is countable. Other refusals: an unknown predicate on an admitted row, and an admitted row with neither clause nor title. The `symbol_present` argument has no field on `spec.Rule` and is not carried. Not wired into a running spec: the caller that reads `knowledge.ReadDir` and merges the rules into `spec.File.Rules` belongs to a later card.

**W-M2.1 Rule kinds and compilation.** Intentional change from the table's `internal/spec/compile.go`: compilation lives in `internal/graph/knowledge/rules.go` as `knowledge.Rules(rows []Row) []spec.Rule`, because knowledge already imports the tree and spec imports nothing; spec keeps the kinds (W-M2.0). `Rules` compiles only rows whose status is approved and whose `predicates[]` field (schema v1, present and empty since W-M1.6) names a predicate the closed list knows; a row with prose and no predicate compiles to nothing (no prose parsing: the proposer of Part B1 is the only path that would write predicates from prose, and it is closed). DECLARED rows compile with HardControl false at nudge; INFERRED rows do not compile (they deliver as lesson lines, never as rules). Tests: `TestRules_ApprovedDecisionWithPredicateCompiles`, `TestRules_CandidateRowCompilesToNothing`, `TestRules_UnknownPredicateRefusedAndReported`, `TestRules_Deterministic`. Depends: W-M2.0.

**W-M2.2 `engine.Diff` and `engine.Conflicts`.** Files: `internal/graph/engine/diff.go` (`DiffOf`), `conflicts.go` (`Conflicts`, dispatch), `boundary.go`, `decision.go`, `duplicate.go`, `protected.go`, `signature.go` (one detector per file under LAW10), `conflicts_test.go`. Interfaces: `DiffOf(g Reader, base, cur graph.Rev, files []graph.ID) Diff` (the records present at cur and absent at base for the files, and the reverse; Touched is the units of Files through Contains); `Conflicts(g Reader, d Diff, rules []Rule) []Conflict` sorted by Kind, Unit, RuleID, Subject. Detectors: boundary_cross, a new edge at d.Rev (Calls, MayCall, Imports, References) from a symbol in a unit matching rule.From to a symbol in a unit matching rule.To, prefix match on the local unit id; decision_contradicted, predicate paths_untouched fires when rule.AppliesTo intersects d.Files or d.Touched, and symbol_present fires when rule.AppliesTo names a symbol and d.Decls adds a callable in a touched unit with no edge to it (PROVISIONAL: the edge set); protected_path, a protect_path rule's Paths contain a file in d.Files (the graph form of the existing spec check, keyed on file ids so the Stop can name it by unit); signature_mismatch, `ArityConflicts` at d.Rev for every callee in d.Decls whose sig attribute differs from the base rev's; duplicate_abstraction, an added func or method whose `Tokens` over name and doc share at least k stems with an existing func or method in another unit that has at least one In Calls edge (k and the exclusion list PROVISIONAL, pre-registered after a replay over twenty merged diffs from this repository's history, which also gives the false-positive rate AC-M2.1 reports). Tests: `TestDiffOf_AddedAndRemovedDecls`, `TestDiffOf_TouchedIsUnitsOfFiles`, `TestConflicts_BoundaryCrossOnNewEdgeOnly`, `TestConflicts_BoundaryPrefixNeverRegexp`, `TestConflicts_DecisionPathsUntouched`, `TestConflicts_DecisionSymbolPresent`, `TestConflicts_ProtectedPath`, `TestConflicts_SignatureMismatchWrapsArity`, `TestConflicts_DuplicateAbstraction`, `TestConflicts_Deterministic`, `TestConflicts_NoneOnThisRepositoryAtHead` (AC-M2.1: this repository's index with the declared boundary below yields zero conflicts on an empty diff and one on a synthetic diff adding an edge from `internal/graph/engine` into `internal/hookio`); `TestEngine_NoLanguageLiterals` unchanged. The declared boundary for AC-M2.1, chosen here from the real import graph and already enforced as a lint by `TestCore_NoAgentKeys`: From `unit:go:internal/graph` (prefix), To `unit:go:internal/hookio`, source spec.json, DECLARED. Depends: W-M2.0.

**W-M2.2 as built (2026-11-11).** Files as above, plus `ruleConflict`, `unitIDOf`, `unitAt` and `removedIDs` in conflicts.go; tests in `diff_test.go`, `conflicts_test.go`, `duplicate_test.go` and `acm21_test.go`. The director's P2 rulings (2026-11-11) applied: R1, symbol_present raises nothing for an added callable whose record at d.Rev is an overlay's, its out-edges being unknown, not absent, so it fires at commit revisions only (`TestConflicts_DecisionSymbolPresent_SilentAtOverlayRev`); R3, DiffOf decides added and removed by node id and edge key, never by attrs, tier or prov, so a record changed in place is in neither list (`TestDiffOf_ChangeInPlaceIsNoChange`); R7(b), duplicate_abstraction skips, per candidate, one the added callable calls by a Calls or MayCall edge at d.Rev or names by short name in its `calls` attr, a same-named function elsewhere being a miss on the safe side (`TestConflicts_DuplicateSkipsCalledCandidate_CommitRev`, `_OverlayRev`). R7(a), the overlay's `doc` and `calls` attrs, is W-M2.5's. Choices: symbol_present's edge set is Calls, MayCall and References, its scope the unit ids the rule names, matched by path segment as boundary's are; a unit is a unit id's own, a symbol's from its id, a file's through Contains at d.Rev, else at d.Base, so a deleted file keeps its unit; protected_path reads the rule's AppliesTo file ids, since `engine.Rule` has no Paths, so whoever builds an `engine.Rule` from a spec protect_path rule resolves its Paths to file ids; signature_mismatch needs both sigs present and different, so a sig appearing is a new spelling, not a change, and on Go it stays dead until an extractor writes arity and argc (M3's). D4: `Key()` keeps an arity conflict's edge key byte for byte and keys every other kind on kind, rule, unit and subject, plus the edge's key when it has an edge (`TestConflict_KeySeparatesEdgelessKinds`); `Conflicts` sorts by the kind's place in ConflictKinds, Unit, RuleID, Subject and edge key, then drops a repeated key. D4's three readers (intent.go:195, recap/rules.go:75, verify/tosteer.go:163) are unchanged, and so is `spec.UnenforcedRuleKinds`: no verdict, ledger row, finding or hit reads `Conflicts` yet, and the card that routes a conflict to one of them owns that reader's default and the list's deletion. AC-M2.1: the fixture twin `TestConflicts_BoundaryCrossOnNewEdgeOnly` passes; the live read `TestConflicts_NoneOnThisRepositoryAtHead` runs only under RASHOMON_ACM21=1 on a synced root. Known limit: a Go overlay asserts Declares alone, so at an overlay revision boundary_cross sees only the edges an overlay writes; the live read adds its edge by hand.

**W-M2.3 First-write moment.** Design forced by the budget fact: the unit brief is computed at UserPromptSubmit, where `graphBrief` has the graph loaded, for the units the brief's five lines name and the frontier's touched units (at most eight), and written as one small file per unit under the store's graph directory (`unitbrief/<session>/<unit digest>.json`: the rendered lines and their keys, no prompt word, `Brief.Evidence`'s rule); at PostToolUse the first landed edit in a unit with such a file reads it (one small read, no graph load) and joins the finding through `hook.SteerEnv.Join`; the file is removed on delivery and the steer log row (key `unitbrief:<unit>`) is the evidence AC-M2.3 reads; a unit with no file is a miss, counted in the record (unitbrief_miss) and never a load. Content, in order (the first fixed by T1b's engine reading: the prompt brief reaches depth 2 from the prompt's seeds and missed the three consumers, while the first write names the unit, from which the consumers are In edges at depth 1): consumers (In Calls, MayCall and Imports from other units, most callers first), rules and decisions whose applies_to reach the unit, lessons by applies_to, the reuse line if the prompt brief had one for this unit; MaxLines 5; the kinds are the W-M1.7 steer kinds as frozen pattern kinds (consumer, decision, lesson, reuse), one finding per line (the W-M1.7 note). `Deltas(prev, cur Brief) []Line` is the set difference on line keys so the unit brief never repeats a prompt-brief line. Files: `internal/graph/engine/unitbrief.go` (`UnitBrief(g Reader, rev graph.Rev, unit graph.ID, prompt Brief, opt Options) Brief`; `Deltas`), `internal/steer/pattern/unitbrief.go`, `cmd/rashomon/graph_unitbrief.go` (precompute at the prompt seam after `graphFinding`; read in `cmdPost` before `Post.Steer`), `internal/hook/steerstep.go` (Join appended beside the blast draft; two lines). Intentional change from the table's `cmd/rashomon/post.go`: the new code is a new file beside it under the preflight seam's shape, because `internal/hook` imports nothing of `internal/graph` and the index read belongs with `graphBrief`. Tests: `TestUnitBrief_ConsumersLeadThenRulesThenLessons`, `TestUnitBrief_DeltasAgainstThePromptBrief`, `TestUnitBrief_FilesHoldNoWordOfThePrompt`, `TestUnitBrief_OncePerUnitPerSession` (the steer log read; a witness row), `TestUnitBrief_MissIsCountedNeverLoaded` (the post path opens no graph.jsonl), `TestPost_JoinRoutesWithinThreeNotes`, K7 `TestK7_PostPathUnitBrief` (p95 at or under 150 ms) and a budgets rss_case `post.unitbrief` (summed peak under 32 MiB). PROVISIONAL: the order after consumers, and whether the reuse line repeats. Depends: W-M2.2 (`Deltas` shares Line keys; Conflicts at post-act rides the same read).

**W-M2.3 as built, second commit (2026-11-11).** Four changes to `engine.UnitBrief`, ordered by the review of the first and a ruling: (1) a budget floor, so consumers no longer starve the rules: after Deltas the brief keeps min(consumers, MaxLines - min(rule lines + lesson lines + reuse line, 3)) consumer lines, then rules, lessons, the reuse line, then the MaxLines cut (`TestUnitBrief_ManyConsumersLeaveThreeForKnowledge`: six consumer files plus a decision, a requirement and a lesson give two consumers then the three records; with no record, five consumers); (2) unit-declared symbols are targets, because gopg declares a symbol with no file (an interface's methods) from the unit node, not from a file, so `unitTargets` now also walks the unit's own Declares edges, deduplicated and in id order (`TestUnitBrief_ConsumerThroughUnitDeclaredSymbol`, control without the unit's Declares edge lists none); (3) References joins Calls, MayCall and Imports in the consumer read, so the unit brief's consumers equal W-M1's at depth 1 (`TestUnitBrief_ConsumerByReference`); (4) by ruling, `Deltas` treats an empty key as no key: a keyless prev line seeds nothing and a keyless cur line is always kept (`TestDeltas_KeylessLinesNeverDedup`). The reuse line still rides last, after Deltas.

**W-M2.4 Conflicts as unit-scoped obligations at nudge.** At PostToolUse (the same read as W-M2.3, once the overlay journal holds the edit) `Conflicts` over `DiffOf(index rev, overlay rev)` become findings at nudge with Kind conflict, Source{spec, the rule id, DECLARED} and Unit; at Stop, `ledgerOf` adds one `Obligation{Kind spec_rule, Unit, Source rule id, Scope session}` per conflict still present at the Stop's rev (`DiffOf` against the tree's overlay of every touched file), state unmet, and none for a conflict the tree no longer shows (met, with the seq of the resolving edit as Evidence); the Stop line names unresolved conflicts by rule id and unit after contradictions and before gates (3.3.6's order); the hook exits 0 and the level stays nudge on every path (R2). Files: `internal/verify/conflicts.go` (new; the producer takes `verify.ConflictRow{Kind, Unit, RuleID, Subject string}`, a value type cmd converts from `engine.Conflict`, so verify imports no graph), the `cmd/rashomon/stop.go` seam, `internal/verify/obligations.go` (the ledger's one new kind path). Tests: `TestObligations_ConflictRowCarriesUnitAndRule`, `TestStop_NamesUnresolvedConflictByRuleID`, `TestStop_ResolvedConflictIsMetWithEvidence`, `TestStop_ConflictNeverBlocks`, `TestLedger_ReplayWithConflicts` (the ledger replays from the record to the same rows, AC-M3.1's shape early). Truth-table rows of PF-IV (nudge, R2) and PF-SP (kinds) move under D161. Depends: W-M2.2.

**W-M2.5 Go overlay and the overlay journal.** `gopg.Resolver.Overlay(rel, content, base, seq)`: `go/parser.ParseFile` with SkipObjectResolution over the content; declarations only (FuncDecl with receiver, GenDecl types, vars, consts) to sym nodes at `Rev{base.Commit, seq}` with attrs name, symkind, sig (the FuncType printed), Tier ALGORITHMIC, Conf 0.8, Certain false, a Declares edge from the file node, and the file node's digest attr the content's `ContentDigest`; content that does not parse yields nil records and a nil error and the brief says unparsed for that file (the overlay says less, never wrong); calls are not resolved (PROVISIONAL after T1b's consumer reading: same-unit name resolution at Conf 0.6 if the consumers line needs it). The journal: `graph.Journal` (`internal/graph/journal.go`, new), one NDJSON file per session under the store's graph directory, appended at act_declared (PreToolUse Write and Edit; the content from tool_input in memory under D160, a Write's content whole, an Edit's new_string spliced into the file's bytes read from disk, nothing of either persisted beyond the records) and at act_landed (PostToolUse, the file re-read, the overlay replacing the declared one by seq); `graph.LoadFile` then `Journal.Replay(store)` at every hook load; removed at session end; bounded (a journal past 4 MiB stops appending and records overlay_journal_full). Tests: `TestGopgOverlay_DeclarationsOnly`, `TestGopgOverlay_TierConfCertain`, `TestGopgOverlay_UnparsedYieldsNothing`, `TestGopgOverlay_DigestIsTheContents`, `TestJournal_AppendReplayEquals`, `TestJournal_LandedReplacesDeclared`, `TestJournal_BoundAndRecorded`, `TestOverlay_DeclaredEditAppearsAtCommitOv1` (AC-M2.4), K7 `TestK7_PreToolUsePathOverlay` (p95 at or under 150 ms; the PreToolUse path parses one file and appends one line, no graph load), a budgets rss_case `pre.overlay`, and the disclosure paragraph for the journal (a write under the store naming the files edited and their declarations). Lane P's package (Part C8): the owner opened lane P on Sonnet 2026-11-05 21:2x, launched after the T1b read under the gates in `docs-out/plans/LANE-P-WORKORDER-2026-11-05.txt`; the lane delivers a frozen patch and the principal commits. Intentional change (owner's word 2026-11-05 21:5x, "dont launch lane P here"): lane P is launched by the owner from a session of their own by gate 6 of the order, never from the M2 session; its worktree `m2-overlay` (branch `as/m2-overlay` at cb3d4296) is prepared and clean; a launch from M2 at 21:5x was stopped within a minute on the owner's word with nothing written (no patch, no cache, no process). Depends: W-M1.3; W-M2.0 for the Diff type.

**W-M2.6 Plan-time check.** `hookio.EventKind` maps a PreToolUse whose tool_name is ExitPlanMode to the plan kind (the adapter is the one place the tool name appears; `TestCore_NoAgentKeys` stays strict); `cmd/rashomon/plan.go` reads tool_input.plan in memory (D160: never stored; planFilePath is not read), names paths and symbols through `intent.Names` and the index's file and symbol nodes, builds `Diff{Touched: the named units, Files: the named files}` with no records, runs `Conflicts` with the boundary and decision rules only, and delivers one finding at nudge (Kind conflict or decision; at most two lines) through PreToolUse `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` of the ExitPlanMode call by default, PostToolUse of the same call being the switch's other position (director, 2026-11-11 16:06 PST: Post reaches accepted plans only); either line is read in the next request, after the call, so before the first write (QA probe 2, t2-probe2-000046, CLI 2.1.322: a PreToolUse additionalContext arrives in the same request as the PostToolUse line, after the tool has run); delivery on a rejected plan: unmeasured at 2.1.322 (QA probe 3; J1-LIVE's rejected plan is the measure of record); the record gets a plan event (shape only: the units and files named, counts). Tests: `TestHookio_ExitPlanModeIsAPlanEvent`, `TestPlan_NamesUnitsFromThePlanText`, `TestPlan_ConflictLineAtNudge`, `TestPlan_HoldsNoWordOfThePlan` (the record and the steer log hold no plan word), `TestPlan_NoIndexYieldsNothing`, and the live AC-M2.1 run. The `claude -p` plan-mode probe is not run: ExitPlanMode is disabled under -p at 2.1.322 (QA probe t2-probe-234104); the channel question was answered on an ordinary tool by QA's probe 2 (above), and AC-M2.1's plan-time clause comes from one tmux-driven interactive session on G's binary after the hook half lands (not shown: NOT MET, a debt row). Depends: W-M2.2.

**W-M2.6 as built (pure parts, 2026-11-11).** Built: (i) `cmd/rashomon/plan.go`, `PlanLines(plan, cwd, sp)` over the plan bytes in memory, writing nothing: `graph.FindIndex(cwd)` and `LoadFile` as `graphBrief` does, names from `intent.Resolve` (paths from `in.Paths`, symbols answered from the index's sym nodes), a named path becomes `graph.FileID` in Files with its unit in Touched (from the index, else the Go resolver's `Unit` for a file on disk the index lacks), `engine.Diff{Rev, Touched, Files}` with no records, and at most one finding of two lines, path-rule lines first; the spec is a parameter (the caller reads it with `hook.DiscoverSpec`, as `brief.go` does), (ii) `internal/graph/engine/rules.go`, `engine.RulesFor(sp *spec.Compiled, rows []knowledge.Row, g engine.Reader, rid string, rev graph.Rev) []engine.Rule` (in engine so QA's J3 judge can import it; `cmd/rashomon/plan.go` calls it), the one function W-M2.4 and QA's J3 judge reuse: the spec's boundary and protect_path rules from Trusted, Managed or Builtin sources plus `knowledge.Rules(rows)`, AppliesTo localised against the index header's repository (a global id of this repository whose node exists at rev becomes its local id, another repository's is dropped, a local-form id passes through; rows are never mutated), its trust set is `engine.RuleTrust`, pinned equal to intent's `trusted` by `TestTrusted_EqualsEngineRuleTrust`, Paths carried over, (iii) `engine.Rule.Paths`, matched by `spec.MatchPath` in `protectedConflicts` and `pathsTouched` for each file in Files, once per file, so a file in Files fires whether or not it is on disk; `PlanLines` takes its paths from `intent.Resolve`, whose `resolvePaths` keeps only paths Lstat finds (`internal/intent/intent.go`), so a file the plan would create reaches no rule at plan time (finding D, 2026-11-11: the hook half adds a planned-file step on the plan path only), (iv) `engine.BoundaryCautions(touched, rules)` in `internal/graph/engine/cautions.go`, one `Caution{RuleID, From, To}` per boundary rule when the plan names a unit under From and another under To, worded as a direction ("forbids edges from X into Y"), never as a conflict, with no obligation and `Conflicts` unchanged. Not built, and still W-M2.6's open end: the `hookio.EventKind` mapping for ExitPlanMode, hook dispatch, the delivery channel and steer record, the plan event, finding D's planned-file step, and the live plan-time line (AC-M2.1's third clause). Only the Go resolver answers a path the index lacks.

Arms U, R (main's binary at the pinned sha) and G (the local `autosteer/m2`'s binary with M2; origin's `as/m2` is the 2026-11-05 docs line 489a4655); one tree sha for all; Sonnet; T1b's caps (150 turns, 15 USD) as T2's pins; the smoke first; the configuration directory's accountUuid equal to `~/.claude`'s before the batch; the meter read twice. Owed to the owner at M2 open: (1) lanes P and S (W-M2.5 then W-M6.1 for P; W-M4.1, W-M4.2 and W-M4.6 against recorded sessions then W-M5.5 for S), proposed in the session at 20:5x with count two, model Sonnet, about 30 to 60 USD per lane-day at list, the meter at 22 percent (ts 1793939910 and 1793939912), launch after the T1b read; ruling 2026-11-05 21:2x: lane P yes on Sonnet; lane S held until the W-M4.2 work order below exists, its model decided by lane P's first frozen patch (merges with minor review changes: Sonnet; a redo: Opus, recorded with the patch as the reason); (2) W-M2.7's owner (Luis, by section 10.2) and its carrier, ruled 2026-11-05 21:5x: not Luis for now, the principal; (3) the T2 prompt wording when W-M2.8 opens. Not safe before W-M2.0 lands: anything on `internal/verify/obligations.go`, `internal/spec`, `internal/graph/engine`; not safe before M2 and M3 close: W-M5.1 (labels need line kinds and predicates) and W-M7.3 (the report needs labels); and every file on the hook path (`steerstep.go`, `preflight.go`, `hooks.json`, `install.go`) has one owner, the principal.

What M2 does not do: tune Scope for the "blast" seed collision T1b's engine reading found (ruling 11 holds until the read; if the read confirms it, the fix is an M1 engine item reopened with its own row, not an M2 item); call a model anywhere; block any tool call; store a plan word or an edit body.

**W-M4.1, W-M4.2 and W-M4.6 work order (written 2026-11-05 21:4x at the owner's word, before M4 opens; intentional change (owner's word, 2026-11-05 21:2x) from this section's rule, so lane S's model can be decided from a bounded order; W-M4.3, W-M4.4, W-M4.5 and W-M4.7 keep their rows and are written when M4 opens; the drift thresholds stay AC-M4.5's pre-registered values and are calibrated by the principal with the owner's labels, never by the lane).**

Facts read at da9e55c8:

- `session.Session` already holds the fold's target shape, {ID, Store, Commit, Goal *Ret, Reqs []*Req, Decisions, Gates, Delivered, Touched []Touch, Knows, Conflicts, Boundary, Seq, Digest, Extract, CapBytes}, with the event methods `Deliver(seq, ids...)`, `Opened(seq, path)`, `Wrote(seq, path)`, `Ran(seq, cmd, passed)`, `MarkBoundary(seq)`, `Stop(seq) Verdict` and `BoundaryBrief()`; `Req{ID, Words *Ret, Tier, Conf, Text, Pred func(*graph.Store, graph.Rev) bool, Holds}`; `Ret` is words by reference {ID, Class, What, Origin, Digest, Bytes, Ref{Path, Line, Off, Len}, Copy}; `New(id, store, commit)` is the only constructor and nothing persists. The fold is a replayer into this type plus a content-free projection out of it, not a new model.
- The record: `store.Open(root)`, `st.ReadRun(sessionID) (*Run, error)` with `Run{Dir, Declarations, Executions, Terminals, Coverage, SpecEvents, AfterHits, ThenChecks}` (records.ndjson; record types declaration, execution, terminal, coverage, gap, forgotten_host, spec_event, after_hit, outcome_join, then_check; schema v5 in `docs/store-schema.json`); `st.ReadSteer(sessionID) (lines [][]byte, skipped int, err error)` (steer.ndjson; `steer.Line{Type, V, ID, AtUnixMS, SessionID, PromptID *string, AgentID *string, Project, Pattern, Kind, Key, PatternVersion, Level, Channel, Event, Attendance, ...}`; a SessionStart briefing line has a null PromptID and is keyed (session_id, "session_start", source, n), so a compact or resume boundary is visible in the steer log wherever ST12's briefing fired, before W-M4.3 adds a record event). The record holds no session_start record and no prompt text (INV-1, D160).
- Boundary vocabulary: `events.Boundary` with forms compact, resume, clear, subagent_start, subagent_stop (W-M1.7); `hookio.EventKind` maps SessionStart's source and the two subagent events (`internal/hookio/events.go:34`); nothing subscribes to them yet (R6, W-M4.3).
- Compliance predicates per line kind exist (`steer.Compliance`, W-M1.7): opened_unit, called_symbol, edited_or_verified, paths_untouched then symbol_present, lesson_own, resolved_by_stop, met, none, returned_to_scope, consumers_verified. They are the requirement predicates for every graph line a session delivered.
- `graph.Predicates` has no continues; the frontier records continues as a field, not an edge (AD1: the session is a fold, not a store row), so the closed list does not grow.
- Fixture sessions that are ours to keep: the harness sessions' install stores (the nine T1 pilot runs, the T1b runs, the smokes) at `/tmp/rk-autosteer/runs/<run>/store/runs/<session id>/{records.ndjson,steer.ndjson}` (read 2026-11-05 21:5x, not under the configuration directories as first written: nine record files, for t1-r1 to t1-r3, t1-g1 to t1-g3, t1b-r1, t1b-g1, which is void at one turn, and t1b-g2; seventeen steer files, the extra ones the judge's replays, unverified; the U arm runs no hooks and leaves no store; the T1b batch adds four R and G runs by its end), content-free by construction (INV-1) and free of any day-job path; AC-M4.1's twenty is more than exist today, so the fixture set is every recorded harness session at lane launch, the test states the count it ran over, and the twenty is a gate the set reaches as T2's runs land; the sessions are copied by exact path into `internal/graph/session/testdata/sessions/<name>/{records.ndjson,steer.ndjson}` with a sha list; none from the owner's own `~/.claude` store.

Interfaces (fixed here; a lane patch that changes one names the reason in its status):

- `session.Frontier` (`internal/graph/session/frontier.go`): {Schema int (1), Session, Repo, Commit, Continues string, Intents []IntentRef{ID, Digest string, StatedAt int64, Status ReqStatus}, Reqs []ReqState{ID, Pred, Arg string, Status ReqStatus, StatedAt, MetAt int64, SupersededBy string}, Decisions []DecisionRef{IDs []string, Action string}, Touched []string (global unit ids), Obligations []ObligationRef{ID, Kind, Unit, State string}, Delivered []LineRef{ID, Key, Kind, Unit string, Seq int64}, Verdicts []VerdictRef{Seq int64, Done bool, Owed int}, Drift Drift, LastBoundary int64}; json names snake_case; `ReqStatus` open, met, dropped, superseded; marshalling deterministic (sorted slices, no maps). The Session keeps Pred as a function; the Frontier keeps the predicate's name and argument, so a frontier is printed, compared and re-delivered without the store (AD17).
- `session.Fold(run *store.Run, lines [][]byte, g *graph.Store, rev graph.Rev, opt FoldOptions) (*Session, Frontier, error)` (`fold.go`): sorts every input by seq (records by seq; lines by at_unix_ms then id), replays into `New(run.SessionID(), g, commit)` through the existing methods (an edit declaration to Wrote; a read declaration to Opened; an execution with its terminal to Ran, passed from the terminal's exit; a delivered steer line to Deliver with its id, keeping key, kind and unit as a LineRef; a session_start line with source compact or resume to MarkBoundary and LastBoundary; a Stop to Stop(seq) and a VerdictRef), then projects the Frontier. `FoldOptions{Through int64 (events at or below this seq; 0 is all), Intent IntentLevel (off, local, share; off is the default and the only level this task implements), Digest func(string) string}`.
- Requirements derived (v1, deterministic, from the record and the steer log only): one Req per graph line the session delivered, id the line's key, predicate the kind's compliance predicate with the line's subject as the argument (unit: opened_unit unit; reuse: called_symbol gid; consumer: edited_or_verified gid; decision: paths_untouched ids; obligation: met id); one Req per declared gate family the session ran (gate_green family); one Req per test name the agent named in a run command (test_named name, from the execution's recorded shape, never its output). Status: open at StatedAt; met at the first seq whose evidence satisfies the predicate against the folded state (opened_unit by a Touch under the unit; called_symbol by an In Calls edge at rev from a touched file's symbol; edited_or_verified by a Touch in the consumer's file or a green run naming it; gate_green by a passed Gate of the family after StatedAt; test_named by a passed run whose shape names the test); dropped only by an explicit drop line (keyed dropped:id, which W-M4.6's narrowing rule writes and no v1 fixture holds, so the test asserts zero drops and no silent closure); superseded when a later prompt brings a Req with the same predicate and a different argument (the earlier stays open and gains SupersededBy). A narrowing prompt never closes an earlier Req (AD18).
- Intents: one IntentRef per prompt id in the steer log (StatedAt the first line's seq, Digest the prompt digest the line carries if any, else the prompt id); content never. Decisions: from delivered decision lines (the line's applies_to ids as IDs; Action leave_alone for paths_untouched, use for symbol_present).
- `session.Drift` (`drift.go`, W-M4.6): {ScopeDivergence, Stagnation, Displacement, ComponentWithoutConsumer, GateSubstitution, PlanMismatch int; Fired []string} from `DriftOf(f Frontier, s *Session, g *graph.Store, rev graph.Rev, th Thresholds) Drift` with `Thresholds{ScopeTurns 3, StagnationTurns 8, StagnationCalls 40, InfraShare 0.6, InfraWindow 20}` as Part B3 pre-registered them; the objective's scope in v1 is the union of the units the delivered unit lines name and the units of the intent's named paths, because objective rows have no importer yet; a signal with no input (PlanMismatch before W-M2.6; ComponentWithoutConsumer without a graph) reads zero and names itself in Fired as no input, never as clear. `DriftLine(d Drift, f Frontier) string` renders the one Stop line under ValidateBriefing's rules (ASCII, no double quote, 160-byte clause); W-M4.4 consumes it and the lane does not wire it. `ReqStatus` transitions are monotone (open to met or superseded; never back).

Tests by name (every one before its code; package-scoped through the slot): `TestFold_IncrementalEqualsFull` (for each fixture session and every seq that carries an event, Fold with Through equals Fold over the inputs cut at that seq, byte-equal frontier JSON; INV-11), `TestFold_IsDeterministic` (inputs shuffled before the sort; equal bytes), `TestFold_HoldsNoPromptBytes` (no T1 prompt content word in any fixture frontier; AC-M4.1), `TestFold_NoTranscript` (transcript unreadable and Intent off: every open Req id with its predicate, every decision by ids, the touched units and the owed obligations survive; AC-M4.1b), `TestFold_BoundaryFromSteerLog`, `TestFold_ReqFromDeliveredLines`, `TestFold_GateGreenMetByLaterPassedRun`, `TestFold_GateGreenOpenWhenRedAfterWrite`, `TestReq_NarrowingLeavesEarlierOpen`, `TestReq_StatusTransitionsAreMonotone`, `TestFrontier_JSONRoundTrip`, `TestFrontier_SchemaIsOne`, `TestDrift_ScopeDivergenceAfterThreeTurns`, `TestDrift_StagnationCountsTurnsAndCalls`, `TestDrift_DisplacementNeedsOpenReqs`, `TestDrift_ComponentWithoutConsumer`, `TestDrift_GateSubstitutionShare`, `TestDrift_NoInputReadsZeroAndSaysSo`, `TestDriftLine_ValidatesAsBriefing`, `TestFold_NoHookioImport` (session imports store, steer, graph and events, never hookio or cmd), and `TestCore_NoAgentKeys` unchanged. Budget: the fold over the largest fixture session under 50 ms (the perf row is the principal's at W-M4.4, when the hook path first reads a frontier; Part C5: a cache file only if the fold exceeds the hook budget).

Files the lane may touch: `internal/graph/session/{fold.go, frontier.go, drift.go, fold_test.go, frontier_test.go, drift_test.go, testdata/sessions/**}`; nothing else. The principal keeps: the record's boundary event and the matchers (W-M4.3), the ST12 delivery (W-M4.4), the subagent frontier (W-M4.5), the calibration (AC-M4.5), the disclosure paragraph for any new write, and the merge. Not decided here: the intent levels local and share (consent; W-M4.4 and the principal), the cache file, the objective rows' importer. What this order buys: W-M4.2 becomes bounded (fixed interfaces, named tests, fixtures we own); the judgment left is the met rule per predicate, which the tests pin. Lane S's model follows lane P's first patch under the rule recorded with the lane P yes (paste-ready orders outside git: `docs-out/plans/LANE-P-WORKORDER-2026-11-05.txt`, `docs-out/plans/LANE-S-WORKORDER-2026-11-05.txt`).

## 7. First vertical slice: T1 on rashomon itself (and the slice sequence T2 to T6)

### 7.1 Why PF-PL7

- It is a PRD row at `none`, so the agent must build, not touch up.
- Main holds a partial implementation to reuse: `internal/platform/callers.go` (package-level PROVEN callers, `Locate`, `Callers`); a parallel implementation elsewhere is the classic miss (S2).
- Its consumers are non-local and unnamed by any prompt: `internal/verify` (`PlatformRead`, `platformObligations`), `cmd/rashomon/report_platform.go`, `cmd/rashomon/laststop.go` (all verified present at 70597cf0).
- Four conventions bind it that no file in the tree states as instructions: PF-PL11 (unknown never zero), LAW10 (100 lines per function, 500 per file), RES-2 (no package-level regexp), D161 (the truth-table row travels with the change), plus the disclosure paragraph for any new read. Verified: the repository tracks no `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.claude/` directory, and the harness runs under its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, so the owner's global instructions are not loaded in either arm. The only way these reach the agent is the steered arm's brief (from the imported ledger and decision rows) or the agent's own reading of `docs-out`, which is outside git and therefore absent from a scratch worktree.
- It exercises the slice requirements the owner listed: ambiguous intent (no file named), architectural discovery (the owning unit), unnamed dependency and abstraction (callers.go, the three consumers), the real channel (UserPromptSubmit through the hook set), measurable planning and implementation change (A1 to A3), obligations (D161, tests), independent verification (hidden oracle, rubric tests), persistent task state (the record and the Stop verdict in the steered arm).

### 7.2 Frozen prompt (proposed; frozen by the pre-registration commit and never edited after a run)

```
Add blast radius to the platform read. For every Go symbol an edit touches, report two numbers: how many callers are proven, and how many are proven or possible. Show the numbers wherever the platform read already appears. Keep the existing tests green and add tests for the new numbers.
```

The prompt names no file, no package, no convention, and does not say "unknown".

### 7.3 Arms and environment (revised by the 11-04 review, Part B5: three arms so the agent, the existing runtime and the graph intelligence are measured apart)

| Arm | Tree | Binary and hooks | Knowledge | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| U unaided | scratch worktree of `origin/main` at a pinned sha (`git worktree add`, never `cp -r`), no `.rashomon/` | none | none | 3 then 10 |
| R runtime | same sha | the main binary at 69fac961 with hooks installed into the harness config dir: record, intent preflight, gates, obligations, Stop verdict; no graph verbs, no `.rashomon/graph.json`; the tree synced with the pinned pgraph before the session, as the shipped plugin's SessionStart hook does (pre-registration adjustment 2026-11-04 23:5x, before any run: `rashomon watch` installs no pgraph hook, so without it arm R would never carry the platform read a developer has) | none | 3 then 10 |
| G AutoSteer | same sha | the branch binary from 70597cf0 plus M1, the same hooks; `pgraph sync` with the arm's own pgraph (T1_G_PGRAPH_SHA), whose sidecars its binary reads; `rashomon graph index .` with `.rashomon/graph.json` listing `.` and `pgraph` as Go units | `.rashomon/knowledge/*.json` imported from `docs-out/plans/FAILURE-LEDGER.txt` and `APPROACH-NOTES.md` at pinned digests | 3 then 10 |

Attribution per metric: runtime effect = R minus U; graph effect = G minus R; total = G minus U. The former ablation arm (G minus knowledge) runs only if G differs from R on the primary outcome and the owner asks which part. Model: Sonnet through `claude -p` with the harness's turn cap and timeout; a fresh config dir per run, logged in once before the batch. Cost: at the 2.50 USD ceiling, three arms at n=3 then n=10 are at most 39 runs, about 98 USD on the meter (ruling 12 set the per-run ceiling; the per-slice total is stated here for the record).

### 7.4 Pre-registered acceptance checks (scripts in `test/live/autosteer/t1/judge/`, committed before the first run; the agent never sees them; the judge runs on each arm's final tree and hook log)

| Id | Measures (all arms unless stated) | Source | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1a discovery by behaviour | before its first edit the agent opened `internal/platform/callers.go` and each consumer file (`internal/verify`'s PlatformRead file, `cmd/rashomon/report_platform.go`, `cmd/rashomon/laststop.go`); one row per item | the harness's tool-call log (Read, Grep, Bash with paths), the instrument the 11-04 evidence used for "read before first edit" | per item opened or not; rates compared across U, R, G |
| A1b discovery in the plan | the agent's text before its first edit names `Callers` or `callers.go` and at least one consumer file | transcript text before the first edit | present or absent |
| A1c channel (G, and R for its preflight) | the first UserPromptSubmit's additionalContext names the unit, the reuse target, a consumer and a knowledge line (PL11, LAW10, RES-2 or D161); an off-task knowledge line counts minus one | hook log, steer log seq (A), graph row ids (G) | per line; reported as the G, A, D chain beside A1a and A2, never as discovery itself |
| A2 reuse by structure | on the arm's final tree, `pgraph sync` then a judge query over `.pgraph/pgraph.log`: a new function in `internal/platform` exists whose PROVEN callers include a symbol in `cmd/rashomon` or `internal/verify`, and no new function outside `internal/platform` and `pgraph` has a `calls` edge to `readLog`, `readDigests` or `DecodeLog` | pgraph on the final tree, the same instrument for every arm | pass or fail |
| A3 consumers by behaviour | the judge replays the hook sequence, one session per oracle symbol (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse and PostToolUse Edit of the symbol's file in a synced copy of `pgraph/testdata/dispatch`, Stop), against a binary built from the arm's final tree with a fresh install store (pre-registration details 2026-11-04 23:5x, before any run: one symbol per session because the Stop prints at most two package lines; the fixture copy carries a CI step naming `go test ./...` and an empty `_test.go` per package, which the Stop's platform row requires on the pinned tree; neither calls an oracle symbol, oracle shapes re-verified unchanged); for the edited symbol two integers (or an integer and an unknown marker) appear on the symbol's line in both renderers the platform read has on the pinned tree, the PostToolUse blast line and the Stop's platform line (pre-registration correction 2026-11-04 23:3x: `rashomon report` has no platform section at 5f851724, so the report text is captured as information, not judged) | arm binary driven by recorded events | both, one or neither renderer; unparsed rows go to a blinded human read |
| A4 correctness (hidden oracle) | `judge/oracle.py` computes from the fixture's `.pgraph/pgraph.log`: proven fan-in = distinct subjects of `calls` to the symbol; inclusive = proven plus subjects of `may-call`; three symbols pre-registered with distinct shapes (proven only; proven and possible so the two numbers differ; none); the numbers parsed in A3 must equal the oracle | oracle script, A3 output | match, mismatch, unparsed |
| A5 unknown never zero (hidden) | a copy of the dispatch fixture adds one file importing `reflect` and calling a method by name (pgraph's fixtures hold no such boundary today, verified); the inclusive number for the affected symbol must not print as a bare zero | A3 replay on the fixture copy | pass, fail, unparsed |
| A6 architecture compliance | `gofmt -l` empty; `go vet ./...`; `TestRubricLAW10_` by name; the RES-2 test; the truth-table test with the PL7 row moved off `none` (D161); the disclosure test for any new read | arm tree through the slot | each pass or fail |
| A7 verification | the judge's `go test ./internal/platform ./internal/verify ./cmd/rashomon` on the final tree (all arms); for R and G: the Stop verdict's claims comparison (no contradicted done) and the obligation ledger state | slot run, record | green or red; contradicted or not |
| A8 cost and latency | cost USD, turns, duration, tool calls from the result json (all arms); hook latency per event from the record (R, G); the prompt-path timing of the graph brief (G) | result json, record | reported; cost per A4-match run; p95 per hook event |

Primary outcome (pre-registered): A2 and A3 and A4 all pass. Secondary: A1a and A1b rates, A5, A6, A7, A8. The attribution table reports U, R, G and the two differences per metric. Stop rule: n=3 per arm, read, then n=10 per arm unless the n=3 mean cost per run exceeds 2.50 USD. No change to the engine, the knowledge rows, the prompt or the judge after any run; a change opens T1b with its own rows. No lift language below n=10; "signal" at best. The T1 task is preserved: none of the changes above found a problem with it.

### 7.5 Evidence rows (one per run; the record file and the milestone PR body carry them)

`arm | run | sha | A1 lines quoted | A2 | A3 | A4 | A5 | A6 | A7 | cost | turns | notes`

### 7.6 The slice sequence after T1 (each on rashomon, each with its own pre-registration; task candidates named now, frozen when the milestone opens)

| Slice | Milestone | Task candidate on rashomon | What it tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| T2 | M2 | PF-PL12 untracked build config warning (touches `pgraph/doc.go` and `internal/platform`; tempts a regexp at package level and a second config walker) | plan-time conflict, first-write unit brief, duplicate-abstraction line |
| T3 | M3 | a signature change in `internal/platform` with the three consumers; the agent is told to "make the read take a context" | consumers-to-verify obligations, Stop verdict independence, done-flag precision |
| T4 | M4 | a two-requirement task (PG12 edit marks plus its report line) under forced compaction (harness context pressure) | frontier re-delivery, requirement status, mistakes against intent |
| T5 | M5 | the T1 task class repeated in a second session after the first session's labels | EARNED lesson delivery, repeated-mistake rate |
| T6 | M6 | two real repositories with a contract between them; candidates: rashomon and its plugin marketplace repository (release contract), or a club volunteer's pair (owner's choice; "Rashomon only" rules out Altrace) | cross-repo consumer edit rate, workspace discovery |

---

### 7.7 Task classes AutoSteer is meant to work for (owner 2026-11-07: generic, every class; W-M1.19 holds at least ten prompts per class)

A task class is a cell of four axes, each read from the prompt alone:

- What the prompt names: nothing (vague); a symptom in prose; a pasted error or test failure; a knowledge id; an identifier; a path.
- The work asked: add; fix; change (refactor); remove; test; measure (performance); explain (question); constrain (negation, "without changing X"); document or operate (non-code).
- The span: one unit; several units (cross-cutting); several tasks in one prompt (multi-task); a work order naming several units (the peer set, W-M1.13); several repositories (M6).
- The state: existing code; greenfield (no unit owns it yet).

The 17 classes of `edge.tsv` are cells of this grid (feature-add, bug-symptom, refactor, question, vague, multi-task, tests-only, performance, delete, cross-cutting, pasted-error, non-go, negation, knowledge-id, named-name, named-path, greenfield); the multi-unit work orders and cross-repo are the two cells the edge set does not hold. What the brief owes per cell is fixed by the cell: a unit line where a unit owns the work and no unit line where none does (vague, non-go, greenfield); a reuse line where an existing abstraction serves the work; consumer lines where the unit has non-local consumers; a knowledge line where a row applies to the unit. A class is accepted when its ten prompts meet AC-M1.5; the product claims only accepted classes and the per-class table is the claim. M1 claims the one-unit and cross-cutting cells on rashomon; multi-unit work orders stay W-M1.13 and cross-repo stays M6.

## 9. Reuse decisions (licenses stated from memory; every one marked "verify at adoption")

| Candidate | Replaces | Cannot provide | Costs (integration, license, maintenance, performance) | Decision | Task |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCIP (`scip-go`, `scip-typescript`, `scip-python`; protobuf index) | a custom PROVEN producer per language | CHA may-call, tiers, overlays, fingerprint identity, knowledge | the language toolchain and the indexer binary at index time (out of band, acceptable under AD3); a Go protobuf reader (`scip` Go bindings, Apache-2.0, verify); indexer version drift | adopt for TypeScript PROVEN; pgraph stays for Go (it exceeds SCIP on CHA and identity) | W-M6.1 |
| Tree-sitter (CLI or Go binding) | a breadth parser for declarations and imports in languages without a PROVEN producer | cross-file resolution, types | cgo binding in the hook binary rejected (static binary, hook path); CLI out of band accepted; MIT (verify); grammar maintenance per language | adopt as the ALGORITHMIC breadth producer, out of band | W-M6.2 |
| graphify (local skill: folder to knowledge graph with INFERRED edges, update, Neo4j export) | nothing in structure (inferred edges may not steer, AD4); a candidate front-end for the offline knowledge proposer over ADR and incident corpora | determinism, tiers, provenance by source row | model tokens; output must be tagged INFERRED; license of the skill (verify) | not in the core; evaluate as an opt-in proposer after M5 under U6 | section 11 |
| Joern (code property graphs, dataflow) | nothing today | hook-path use (JVM, seconds to minutes) | heavy runtime; Apache-2.0 (verify) | reject for now; revisit for taint and dataflow lines on a customer scenario | section 11 |
| Graphiti (temporal knowledge graph, LLM extraction, Neo4j) | the judgment store | local, deterministic, no-database, tiers | a database and a model in the loop | reject; borrow bi-temporal validity (`valid_from`, `invalid_at`) on knowledge rows | W-M5.3 |
| Aider repo-map (PageRank over tree-sitter tags) | Rank | tiers, knowledge, conflicts | Python; Apache-2.0 (verify) | borrow the algorithm (personalized PageRank from the touched set) as a Rank alternative if Select precision fails AC-M1.1 at scale; no dependency | W-M6.6 (conditional) |
| OpenHands | nothing as a component | n/a | n/a | reference for the canonical event schema and as an adapter target | W-M7.1, W-M7.7 |
| OPA and Rego | spec rule kinds | a closed, auditable rule set; small binary | tens of megabytes linked into the hook binary; Rego authoring cost; Apache-2.0 (verify) | reject for the core; rule kinds stay a closed enum; revisit if a customer brings Rego policies | section 11 |
| Import-boundary linters (depguard, go-arch-lint, dependency-cruiser, import-linter) | hand-written boundary rules | n/a | read their config files only, no linking | adopt their configs as DECLARED boundary sources, Go first | W-M2.7 |
| `go/packages`, `go/doc`, `go/parser` (stdlib and x/tools, already in pgraph) | n/a | n/a | none new | keep | W-M1.1, W-M2.5 |

What AutoSteer owns because it is the differentiated intelligence: the tiered, provenance-carrying graph with overlays; Scope, Traverse, Rank, Check Conflicts, Select; the delivery moments and budgets; the record-based verification; the session fold; the labeled learning loop. Everything in the table above is a producer or a borrowed algorithm behind the Resolver interface.

## 10. Engineering ownership, integration and reporting

### 10.1 Agents

- One principal session (this one, Opus) owns the plan, the backlog, integration, merges and every live experiment. It implements the spine inline (contract types, index, knowledge, engine, `cmd` wiring, every slice).
- Under strategy B (Part C8): two Sonnet implementer lanes with disjoint package ownership (lane P producers; lane S session fold and learning), delivering frozen patches with tests that the principal reviews and commits; one Sonnet QA pass per PR. Each lane is proposed with count, model and estimated cost, the meter read twice, and launched only on the owner's yes; no workflows; no swarm. Strategy A (no lanes) is the fallback at any time.
- Luis: vertical owner of the PRD rows inside the milestone sets of section 2.3; his PRs land through carriers at task boundaries; the owner communicates the re-sequencing (U7).

### 10.2 Luis's rows by milestone set

M1: PL2, PL7 (through T1), PL11 (first half). M2: PF-SP rows, PF-IV rows. M3: PG1 to PG13, PL8, PL12. M4: ST12 continuity, HD11. M5: HD1 to HD17 except HD13. M6: PL5, PL6, PL10, PL13. M7: HD7, HD9, PL14, PF-BR, PF-SH. Any Luis PR touching `internal/graph`, `internal/platform` or `internal/graph/session` names a section 6 id in its body.

### 10.3 Branches and worktrees

- Integration branch: `autosteer/m2` from M2's open (6.2, "M2 opened at 072e3e36", ruling 8), created from main's merge commit of PR #283, or from 072e3e36 if the merge has not landed when the registration commit is ready (main is then merged in at the first task boundary under the next bullet). Task branches `as/m2-<task>` from it; merged back by fast-forward or merge commit after the task gate. M1's integration branch `autosteer/contracts-v2` (worktree `contracts-v2`; task branches `as/m1-<task>`) stays at 072e3e36 as M1's record. `as/m2` (489a4655) and `as/m2-overlay` (cb3d4296) are retired once their block is ported, and not deleted.
- Merge `origin/main` into the integration branch at every task boundary, gated by `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, the pgraph build and the changed packages' tests through the slot; more than three conflicts triggers a review of Luis's set.
- PR to main only at a milestone's demonstration gate, carrying the evidence rows; merge on the owner's word; push only on the owner's word.
- Scratch worktrees for live runs: `git worktree add` at a pinned sha, removed by exact path after the judge runs; never `cp -r`; never a bare stash.
- The three dirty worktrees (`autosteer-55`, `brief1`, `brief1-mac`) are inventoried under W-DEBT.3 and untouched until the owner rules.

### 10.4 Quality gates

- Per task: tests by name through the slot; `gofmt`, `go vet`; LAW10 and RES-2 tests; the disclosure paragraph; the truth-table rows (D161); a budgets row when the hook path changes; sweep anchors updated when a guard moves.
- Per milestone: the live slice at n=10 pre-registered; K7 rows; AC rows green; QA review; the record file's evidence section updated before the PR is opened; `set -uo pipefail` and `ls-remote == sha` before any GitHub write.

### 10.5 Reporting

- The per-session state file `docs-out/plans/AUTOSTEER-EXECUTION-RECORD.md` (outside git) keeps a state line, the open task id, the slot order and the evidence rows; this plan (versioned under U1) keeps the objective, matrix, decisions, backlog and status.
- Progress to the owner in the seven-item form only: capability, code path, real agent interaction, independently verified outcome, baseline comparison, limitations, unfinished requirements. Never commit or line counts.
- Any conflict between work and this plan is flagged in the state line as either "intentional change (owner's word, date)" or "drift (reverted)". There is no third category.

## 12. Definition of product readiness (production-grade, vision section 10)

AutoSteer is ready when all of the following hold at once, each with evidence rows a reader can rerun:

1. C1 to C9 demonstrated live on rashomon and on at least one external repository, each at n=10 or more per arm with pre-registered judges, with the measured difference reported in signal language and the cost per correct outcome.
2. All 44 truth-table rows `built` or deferred by the owner's written word; every row's evidence names the code path, the test and a live seq.
3. The twelve scenarios each have a recorded, replayable run with a judge script.
4. Readiness A to J gates green per section 8, including K7 rows at 50K records, the hook budgets, and a blinded precision at or above 0.9 for done flags and labels.
5. Two PROVEN languages and one breadth producer; a two-repository workspace; a monorepo with partial index.
6. No model call in any hook; every note names its tier; every record says G, A or D; disclosure names every read; consent gates content.
7. Install in nine lines on a fresh machine; `/autosteer:report` and `/autosteer:why` ship in the plugin; troubleshooting and degradation paths documented; a tagged release.
8. The learning loop has promoted at least one EARNED lesson through a holdout and retired at least one refuted lesson, in production records.
9. The plan's status section reads the same as the tree: no `experimental` status remains.

## 13. Rulings (owner, 2026-11-04, on the twelve decisions; the two left to the principal are marked "picked")

1. Plan location: `docs/autosteer/PLAN.md`, `docs/autosteer/TRACE.md` and `docs/autosteer/VISION.md` are committed as the single exception to the no-commit-docs rule. VISION.md is the 11-04 vision verbatim (`~/autosteer_vision.md`), read at every session start; the 10-31 end-state vision stays referenced from PLAN.md's Spec line. Nothing else under `docs/` or `docs-out/` is exempt.
2. Keep 70597cf0.
3. PR #281: merge now (picked). Reason: it is green and mergeable, W-M3.5 builds on it, and every week it waits is another merge of Luis's train through its six files. The three surviving mutants are coverage debt (W-DEBT.1), not behaviour, and stay tracked. The merge is a GitHub write: `set -uo pipefail`, `ls-remote` equals the PR head sha, then `gh pr merge`; never a force-push.
4. T1 baseline is the unaided agent; ablation only after signal at n=3.
5. Go facts enter through pgraph's sidecars; no pgraph import (AD12).
6. Identity migration in `internal/platform` in M6 (W-M6.4).
7. Agent-key lint tripwire in M1 (W-M1.7); adapters in M7 (W-M7.1, W-M7.7).
8. Luis re-sequenced into milestone sets (section 10.2); the owner tells him.
9. The model-assisted proposer stays closed until after M5, with the owner's reservation: if AC-M1.1 fails (the brief's unit line misses on the five pre-registered prompts), the resolver is the next experiment and this ruling is reopened. Recorded as the trigger in section 11.
10. The harness moves into `test/live/autosteer/` with fixtures by sha (AD13).
11. The T1 prompt (section 7.2) and the judge rules (section 7.4) are approved as written; the pre-registration commit freezes them before the first run.
12. n=3 cost ceiling: 2.50 USD mean per run (picked). Basis: the Python fixtures measured 0.305 to 0.472 USD per run; rashomon is a larger Go tree where Sonnet reads eight to fourteen files before its first edit and must run `go test`, so two to five times that is the expected band. Above 2.50 mean at n=3, n=10 waits for the owner's word. At the ceiling, T1 costs at most 20 runs times 2.50 plus 5 ablation runs, about 62 USD on the meter.
13. The T6 fixture (section 7.6) is decided when M6 opens, not now.
14. Report (owner, 2026-11-05 22:3x, after reading `rashomon report --session 28d65377`): the default `rashomon report` is the user-facing outcome and answers, with evidence for every claim, what the user was trying to achieve, what AutoSteer did to help, what it independently verified, and what it learned for next time; its four measurable capabilities are goal continuity, corrective steering, verified completion and compounding project knowledge; every intervention carries a trail (what the agent was about to do, what AutoSteer supplied, what changed, how it was verified); the agent's own account is never presented as a verified outcome; "I did not need to intervene" is an acceptable report; no tokens-saved or mistakes-prevented claim without evidence. Today's output (declarations, executions, failed calls, hosts, chains, reconciliation) moves whole to `rashomon report --audit`. Priority before any report polish: one complete loop (goal, relevant constraint, steer at the moment, observed decision, verified outcome, retained knowledge) with the report generated from that loop's evidence. Facts read at the ruling: the reviewed session was still open (its transcript written at 06:04Z), so "no session end recorded" names an open session, which the report words as a gap; its project (docs-out/plans) has no Go gates, so "checks: none expected" is honest; the T1b rows measure this gap (delivery works: g2 A1a 4/4, A1c reuse true; no changed decision verified against the oracle: A3 neither in five of five valid rows). Extends D162 and ruling 10-29 (two surfaces, evidence never a score).

15. Session data in the graph (owner, 2026-11-05 23:0x, after the read of t1b-g2's store against its transcript): the problem is ease of access and one central location, which is the graph, not scattered files; the consent chain and the disclosure are not a constraint on the data design. Facts read at the ruling (t1b-g2, 183 calls, 551 record lines): the store keeps a prompt_id and no prompt; the program name and a keyed digest of a command and no command text (the schema's command field has no writer under internal/); exit codes on failed calls only, so on none of the 50 Bash calls; 0 bytes of tool output (23 runner outputs read in memory for a gate verdict and dropped, while the transcript holds 123 results, 287 KB); no path on any of the 68 Reads; a path and a sha256 per edit and no diff; two claims as class, verdict and an HMAC of the quote; 30 steer rows with the facts null on every one and the delivered words on none; and the report built from them says "go test ran with other arguments", "17 test runs (0 ok, 0 failed)" and "11 mistakes missed, found later unknown (no trace is recorded)". The session's data is scattered over records.ndjson, steer.ndjson, coverage.ndjson, recap.json, the hook log and Claude Code's transcript, and the report joins them by seq at read time. Ruling: every session's content lands in the graph as nodes and edges in the same id space as the code index, with the words kept: the prompt (its text); each call (tool, the command line, cwd, exit status, duration, the output or its tail, a runner's output whole); each read (the path, an edge to the file node); each edit (the path and the diff, edges to the file and the symbols it touched); each claim (the quoted text, class, verdict, edges to the calls that evidence it); each note AutoSteer delivered (the words as delivered, the event, the calls that evidenced it and the call that followed); each verify verdict (gates and states); each lesson, with edges to what it was learned from. One store and one query path: the report, the brief, the learn loop and the boundary brief read the same graph, and nothing an answer needs is left in a file only the transcript holds. Overrides: D160's storage clause (the prompt read in memory and never stored) is withdrawn; its no-model-in-hooks clause stands (AD5). The two W-M1.9 tests that pin a prompt-free evidence file (`TestEvidence_HoldsNoWordOfThePrompt`, `TestGraphBrief_EvidenceFileHoldsNoWordOfThePrompt`) pin the withdrawn clause and go with it. The record-scope gate (`store.GateDeclaration`, `GateExecution`) and the consent screen's RECORDED and NEVER STORED paragraphs describe the old design; the screen's text changes in the same commit as the writer so that it says what the binary does (a one-paragraph edit plus the TRU pins; not a gate on the work, and not a reason to delay it). Caps stay, because the hook budget is their reason, not consent: a command line cut at the existing byte cap, output tails at a fixed cap with the runner's output whole, under the schema's existing _cut markers. Engineering shape (principal's read from T1b, the M2 detail the order asked for): (a) the hook path keeps appending one line per event, because an append under a hook that may be killed is durable and a graph rewrite per call is not, but the lines are graph records (`graph.Node` and `graph.Edge` under persist.go's schema) in the install store's graph file for the repository, keyed by rid in the same `GlobalID` space as `.rashomon/graph.jsonl`, so there is one `Store` at read time and no fold; (b) every source is already in the hook's hands and only has to be kept: tool_input and tool_response are scanned keys of the payload (`hookio.KeyNames`), the final message is read from the transcript by the claims reader, the prompt by `intent.Resolve`, the delivered words by the renderer; (c) session predicates join the closed list (asks, acts, reads, edits, runs, claims, evidenced_by, delivered, followed_by; `TestPredicates_Closed` updated) with node kinds session, prompt, call, claim, note, verdict; (d) strangler order: the graph writer lands beside the record writer, the report (ruling 14) and `--audit` move to the graph first, then verify, recap and learn, and the record, steer and evidence-file writers retire when no reader is left, so no reader is ever broken and no double bookkeeping outlives the move; (e) `.rashomon/graph.jsonl` stays the code index a `graph index` run regenerates from the tree, and session records never go into a file a regeneration overwrites. Size at the g2 scale: under 2 MB of content a session, less than the code index (7.7 MB). Effort: writer 4-6 h (the fields exist in the payload and the schema), predicates and kinds 2-3 h, the report on the graph 6-9 h, the screen and pins 1-2 h; retiring the record writer is costed when the last reader moves. Placement: the first M2 task before W-M2.3 (the first-write moment needs the read edges and the delivered words to judge itself), to be written into as/m2's 6.2 by the M2 session, since 6.2's step detail lives on that branch and this branch's PLAN carries the ruling only; T1b's harness, engine and pins do not move until the batch and the n=10 question close (section 7.4; a byte change opens T1c).

Execution order on exit from planning: (1) merge #281; (2) copy this plan to `docs/autosteer/PLAN.md`, the vision to `docs/autosteer/VISION.md`, start `docs/autosteer/TRACE.md` with the section 2 matrix and the section 13 rulings, commit on `autosteer/contracts-v2`; (3) W-M1.0 and the T1 pre-registration commit; (4) W-M1.1 onward in section 6.1 order; no push until the owner's word.

## 17. On `/batch` (asked 2026-11-04)

Verified in this install (Claude Code 2.1.280): no skill or command named `batch` is installed under `~/.claude/skills`, `~/.claude/commands` or the plugin caches. `/batch` is Claude Code's built-in fan-out: one instruction sharded across many subagents in isolated worktrees. It is the same thing as a workflow for the meter and for the no-fan-out rule, so it runs on Sonnet or Haiku only and only after a proposal with count, model and cost.

Where it cannot speed this plan: M1's critical path is a dependency chain (symbols sidecar, then Snapshot, then resolver, then index, then importer, then the slice), where each task fixes an interface the next consumes; a shard cannot start before its predecessor's interface exists. The live slice's runs are `claude -p` processes the harness already runs without agents.

Where it fits, later and only by proposal: mechanical, independent, shardable edits such as sweep-anchor updates across the mutant files after a guard moves, disclosure paragraphs across many reads in M7, or the breadth producer's per-language fixtures in M6. The real speedups available now are structural: merge #281 today, two tasks per Go slot window (M4's reading and design while M1's Go runs), and writing the T1 judge scripts while the n=3 batch runs. Part C supersedes this paragraph's framing with a measured topology.
